Uterine Leiomyoma

Complex MONDO:0007886 Pathograph 30 Show in embeddings browser Benign uterine neoplasm Smooth muscle tumor

Uterine leiomyomas are benign monoclonal smooth muscle tumors that arise from a single myometrial cell acquiring a driver mutation in MED12, HMGA2, or FH. Ovarian estrogen and progesterone then drive smooth muscle proliferation and abundant extracellular matrix deposition during each ovulatory cycle. Progressive tumor growth distorts the uterus, producing abnormal uterine bleeding, bulk symptoms, and reproductive complications.

Subtypes

3
MED12-mutant leiomyoma
MED12 hgnc:11957 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in MED12 (hgnc:11957). hgnc:11957 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
The dominant molecular subtype, defined by a recurrent somatic exon-2 point mutation in MED12 disrupting the CDK8/CDK19 kinase module of the Mediator complex. These tumors tend to be multiple rather than solitary, are found even at small size, are more frequent in Black women, and carry a distinctive ECM-organization and Wnt/beta-catenin transcriptional program. Reported frequency spans 50-80% of tumors depending on cohort and detection method.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37668348 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Among the driver gene mutations identified in UFs, mutations in MED12, a component of the cyclin-dependent kinase (CDK) Mediator module, are the most common and observed in 50-80% of UFs."
Establishes MED12-mutant tumors as the most common molecular subtype at 50-80%.
HMGA2-rearranged / HMGA2-overexpressing leiomyoma
HMGA2 hgnc:5009 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in HMGA2 (hgnc:5009). hgnc:5009 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
The second subtype, defined by 12q14-15 rearrangement or overexpression of the architectural transcription factor HMGA2. Around 10% of fibroids overall, but markedly enriched in the cellular leiomyoma histologic variant, where HMGA2 overexpression was found in 36.5% and HMGA2 rearrangement in 13.2% of cases. Largely mutually exclusive with MED12 mutation.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34626221 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"36.5% showed HMGA2 overexpression at the IHC level, associated with increased mRNA expression in 14/14 cases. The rearrangement of the HMGA2 gene was detected in 13.2%."
Quantifies HMGA2 overexpression and rearrangement in the cellular leiomyoma variant where this subtype concentrates.
PMID:38326302 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Notably, the majority of these mutations are mutually exclusive, and integrative data analysis reveals mutation-specific distinct driver gene expression programs and biomarkers in UF"
Supplies the citation for the mutual-exclusivity claim in this subtype's description, and for the premise that each driver class has its own expression programme - which is what makes these molecular subtypes rather than co-occurring lesions.
FH-deficient leiomyoma
FH hgnc:3700 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in FH (hgnc:3700). hgnc:3700 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
An uncommon but clinically consequential subtype defined by biallelic loss of fumarate hydratase, recognizable on routine histology by a cluster of features (staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern oedema, bizarre nuclei, eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes, eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules) and confirmable by loss of FH or gain of 2SC staining. It matters out of proportion to its frequency because a large fraction of cases carry a germline FH variant - 31 of 80 tumors (39%) in the reference series, or 52% of those in which sequencing found a variant at all - which makes the patient and her relatives HLRCC kindred at risk of renal cell carcinoma.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37263921 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"More than four histologic features, including staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern edema, bizarre nuclei, oval nuclei arranged in chains, prominent eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes and eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules were observed in 98.5% (67/68) patients."
Establishes the recognizable histologic signature that defines this subtype in practice, present in 98.5% of the series.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

3
If MED12 exon-2 mutation is a gain-of-function driver present in most fibroids, why do MED12 wild-type tumors grow faster than mutant ones?
CONTROVERSY OPEN med12_mutation_growth_paradox
Attached to
pathophysiology#Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation genetic#MED12 somatic exon-2 mutation
The entry records MED12 mutation as a gain-of-function driver, which is what the review literature says, and that reading invites the inference that the mutation drives growth. A 44-tumor series reports the opposite for growth specifically: wild-type leiomyomas have higher growth potential and higher MED12-associated CDK8 kinase activity than mutant ones, and CDK8 inhibition suppresses wild-type cell growth in primary culture. One way to reconcile these is that the mutation is required for tumor initiation while wild-type tumors, once initiated by some other route, retain more intact Mediator-kinase output and therefore grow faster. That reconciliation is not established, and an entry that recorded only the gain-of-function label would hide the tension rather than resolve it.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41493967 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Of the 44 leiomyomas tested, 11 MED12 wild-type leiomyomas without preoperative GnRH agonist treatment had significantly higher CDK8 activity than nine GnRH agonist-treated MED12 wild-type leiomyomas and 15 leiomyomas with MED12 mutations without GnRH agonist treatment."
The measurement behind the controversy: CDK8 activity is highest in untreated wild-type tumors, lower in mutant ones.
PMID:41493967 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Treatment of primary cultured MED12 wild-type cells with CDK8 inhibitors significantly inhibited cell growth and increased apoptosis."
Shows the CDK8 activity is functionally load-bearing for growth in the wild-type tumors, which is what makes this a mechanistic question rather than a measurement artifact.
Which mechanism dominates fibroid-associated heavy menstrual bleeding: rupture of structurally deficient pseudocapsule neovessels, dilated venous lakes from mass effect, or TGF-beta-3-mediated endometrial hemostatic failure?
OPEN QUESTION OPEN hmb_dominant_mechanism
Attached to
pathophysiology#Aberrant Pseudocapsule Angiogenesis and Venous Ectasia pathophysiology#Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure phenotypes#Heavy Menstrual Bleeding
All three mechanisms are separately evidenced and this entry curates all three, but their relative contribution is unsettled and the candidate explanations make different predictions. The pseudocapsule account predicts bleeding should track fibroid location, since only intramural fibroids develop a pseudocapsule. The venous-lake account was originally attributed to interstitial pressure and later reattributed to growth factors enlarging the veins. The endometrial account is location-independent and predicts bleeding even from fibroids that do not distort the cavity. Distinguishing them matters clinically, because tranexamic acid and the levonorgestrel system act on the endometrial arm while embolization and ablation act on the vascular one.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These aberrations in vascular architecture and network could explain the heavy menstrual bleeding observed. However, other theories have been proposed such as venous trunks, or venous lakes caused by the blocking of normal blood flow by uterine fibroids, or the increased local action of..."
The source itself frames these as competing theories under review rather than as a settled mechanism, which is the state this discussion records.
Does a Tsc2-driven rat tumor tell us anything about how a MED12-, HMGA2- or FH-driven human fibroid starts?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN eker_rat_driver_mismatch
Attached to
pathophysiology#Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation animal_models#Rat
The Eker rat is the workhorse spontaneous model and its tumors look and behave like human fibroids at the tissue and hormonal level, so results from it are routinely read as being about fibroid biology generally. But its lesion is a germline Tsc2 mutation acting through mTOR, and none of the recurrent human somatic drivers is present. The mismatch is therefore not that evidence is missing but that evidence exists in a system whose initiating genotype is wrong, which is the distinction between a knowledge gap and a model mismatch. The practical consequence is that the developmental-exposure and vitamin D results obtained in this strain support the environmental records in this entry at the level of myometrial stem cell DNA repair, and do not support any claim about the human driver mutations themselves.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41810407 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Current uterine fibroid animal models cannot fully reproduce the complex pathological process of human ULs, so optimization of current pathological models is needed."
The review's own statement that no current animal model fully reproduces human fibroid pathology, which is the general form of this specific mismatch.
⚙

Pathophysiology

9
Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation
Uterine leiomyomas (fibroids) originate as monoclonal tumors from a single myometrial smooth muscle cell (or a myometrial stem cell acquiring tumor-initiating features) that acquires a recurrent somatic driver mutation. The most common driver is a MED12 exon-2 mutation (~70-77%), followed by HMGA2/HMGA1 overexpression or chromosomal rearrangement (~10%, 12q14-15), biallelic FH (fumarate hydratase) loss defining the FH-deficient subtype (also seen in hereditary leiomyomatosis and renal cell cancer, HLRCC), with rarer collagen IV (COL4A5/COL4A6) deletions also reported. Heterozygous MED12 mutations disrupt the CDK8/CDK19 kinase module of the Mediator complex, altering the chromatin landscape, enhancer engagement, genomic stability, and progesterone responsiveness. Scoping decision, recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight: COL4A5/COL4A6 deletion and the SRCAP-complex class are named here but are deliberately not given has_subtypes entries. Both are recurrent but rare relative to the three modelled classes, neither has a distinct pathophysiology arm this entry could populate beyond the shared ECM output, and neither changes management. A curator with a collagen-IV-specific mechanism or frequency source should add the subtype then.

HMGA2 hgnc:5009 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves HMGA2 (hgnc:5009). hgnc:5009 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. FH hgnc:3700 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves FH (hgnc:3700). hgnc:3700 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Genetic context variant_origin: SOMATIC functional_impact_category: GAIN_OF_FUNCTION
This block describes the MED12 arm only, which is the majority route and the one the node's evidence characterizes. It does not describe FH, whose lesion is biallelic loss of a tumor suppressor (LOSS_OF_FUNCTION) and is germline in roughly half of FH-deficient tumors - see the separate FH node and the FH-deficient subtype. It does not describe HMGA2 rearrangement either, which is a structural event rather than a point-mutation gain of function. The slots are single-valued, so restricting them to one arm and saying so is more accurate than picking a value that is wrong for two of the three genes.
Wnt/beta-catenin signaling dysregulation GO:0016055 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased Wnt/beta-catenin signaling dysregulation, annotated with Wnt signaling pathway (GO:0016055). Animal models
Eker rat (germline Tsc2 mutation) Rat fails to recapitulate The model does not reproduce the human initiating lesion. Its tumors start from a germline Tsc2 mutation present in every cell, where human fibroids start from a somatic driver acquired by one myometrial cell, so the monoclonal-origin and driver-genotype claims of this node are outside what the model can support.
Downstream
Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation The transformed clonal cell becomes dependent on estrogen and progesterone: progesterone (via PGR) drives paracrine WNT ligand secretion that stimulates tumor stem cell proliferation.
Fumarate Accumulation in FH-Deficient Leiomyoma In the FH-deficient subtype the driver lesion is biallelic loss of the tricarboxylic-acid-cycle enzyme fumarate hydratase, so the initiating event is metabolic rather than transcriptional: fumarate accumulates and succinates cysteine residues, which is the branch this edge names.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:40214304 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The most common somatic mutations in fibroids affect the Mediator complex subunit 12 (MED12; 77%) and high-mobility group AT-hook 2/1 (HMGA2/1; 10%) genes."
Establishes MED12 (77%) and HMGA2/1 (10%) as the dominant recurrent somatic drivers initiating fibroid tumors.
PMID:40214304 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Heterozygous mutations in MED12, a chromatin-associated protein, disrupt the attached CDK8 kinase module in the Mediator complex. MED12 mutations are associated with increased genomic instability, altered chromatin landscape and enhancer engagement, and increased responsiveness to progesterone."
Describes the molecular consequence of MED12 driver mutations on the Mediator kinase module and progesterone responsiveness.
PMID:37668348 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Among the driver gene mutations identified in UFs, mutations in MED12, a component of the cyclin-dependent kinase (CDK) Mediator module, are the most common and observed in 50-80% of UFs."
Independent review confirming MED12 as the most common driver, present in 50-80% of fibroids.
+ 1 more reference
Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation
Estradiol and progesterone are essential for fibroid growth. During each ovulatory cycle the myometrium proliferates under ovarian steroid stimulation. Progesterone, acting through its nuclear receptor (PGR) in differentiated fibroid cells, activates paracrine secretion of WNT ligands, cytokines, and growth factors that drive a small stem cell population to proliferate. Fibroids rarely arise before menarche and frequently regress after menopause, underscoring steroid hormone dependence. The excessive matrix these proliferating cells and the adjacent tumor-associated fibroblasts lay down is modelled as the separate downstream node "Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening"; this node is the cell-population expansion alone.

Animal models
Eker rat (germline Tsc2 mutation) Rat partially recapitulates Reproduces spontaneous, hormone-dependent uterine smooth muscle tumorigenesis in an intact animal, which is what makes it usable for gene-environment and chemoprevention work no in vitro system can do.
Downstream
Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening The expanded clone and the tumor-associated fibroblasts recruited alongside it secrete the collagen-rich matrix that gives the tumor its bulk and its name.
Aberrant Pseudocapsule Angiogenesis and Venous Ectasia The growing clone induces its own blood supply, organized as a vascularized pseudocapsule rather than by the hypoxia-driven route used by malignant tumors.
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:40214304 SUPPORT Other
"Progesterone, via its receptors in differentiated fibroid cell populations, activates the production of Wingless-type MMTV integration site family (WNT) ligands, cytokines, and other growth substances to act on adjacent stem cells in a paracrine fashion to support tumor growth."
Defines the progesterone-driven paracrine WNT mechanism supporting fibroid stem cell proliferation.
PMID:40214304 SUPPORT Other
"Both the mutated smooth muscle cells and the adjacent tumor-associated fibroblasts lay down excessive quantities of extracellular matrix, providing a unique feature that led to naming these tumors "fibroids.""
Establishes that mutated smooth muscle cells and tumor-associated fibroblasts deposit the excessive ECM characteristic of fibroids.
PMID:37668348 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Gene expressions related to ECM organization and collagen-rich ECM components are upregulated, and impaired Mediator kinase activity and dysregulation of Wnt/β-catenin signaling are identified in MED12-UFs."
Links MED12 mutation to upregulated ECM-organization/collagen genes and Wnt/beta-catenin dysregulation.
+ 2 more references
Fumarate Accumulation in FH-Deficient Leiomyoma
In the fumarate-hydratase-deficient subtype, biallelic FH loss blocks the tricarboxylic-acid cycle at the fumarate-to-malate step, so fumarate accumulates and non-enzymatically succinates protein cysteine residues, forming the S-(2-succino)-cysteine adduct that immunohistochemistry detects. This node is the accumulation step alone; what the accumulated fumarate then does is the downstream pseudohypoxia node, matching the way the module it conforms to separates the two. The subtype is defined by the same chemistry whether the first FH hit is germline or somatic, and the germline case is hereditary leiomyomatosis and renal cell cancer, curated separately as Hereditary_Leiomyomatosis_and_Renal_Cell_Cancer.
myometrial smooth muscle cell CL:0002366 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves myometrial smooth muscle cell, annotated with myometrial cell (CL:0002366). CL:0002366 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.

fumarate hydratase activity GO:0004333 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this molecular function This pathophysiological event involves decreased fumarate hydratase activity (GO:0004333). GO:0004333 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Downstream
Pseudohypoxic HIF Stabilization in FH-Deficient Leiomyoma Accumulated fumarate competitively inhibits the 2-oxoglutarate-dependent HIF prolyl hydroxylases, which is the step from oncometabolite to pseudohypoxia.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37263921 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Based on the Sanger sequencing results, the cases were divided into germline variant group (31 cases), somatic variant group (29 cases) and no variant group (20 cases)."
An 80-case FH-deficient leiomyoma series in which the FH lesion is germline in roughly as many tumors as it is somatic, supporting the claim that the subtype is defined by FH loss rather than by how the first hit was acquired.
PMID:36981015 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Loss of function variant of FH gene inactivates the Kreb's cycle enzyme activity and predisposes individuals with such variant to the development of HLRCC."
Establishes that the pathogenic mechanism of an FH variant in uterine leiomyoma is loss of the Krebs-cycle enzyme activity, which is the lesion this node describes.
Pseudohypoxic HIF Stabilization in FH-Deficient Leiomyoma
With the HIF prolyl hydroxylases inhibited by fumarate, HIF-alpha escapes degradation and a hypoxia transcriptional programme runs in a normoxic cell. This is the FH subtype's distinctive route to the tumor's distorted, staghorn vasculature. Read this alongside the angiogenesis node, which records HIF-1-alpha as absent from uterine fibroids. The two are not in conflict but the boundary matters: that observation comes from unselected fibroids, which are overwhelmingly MED12- or HMGA2-driven, and it is what establishes that the common fibroid does NOT use the hypoxia/VEGF route. Pseudohypoxia is specific to the uncommon FH-deficient subtype, where it is driven by a metabolite rather than by low oxygen. A reader who took the pseudohypoxia claim to apply to fibroids generally would have it backwards.
myometrial smooth muscle cell CL:0002366 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves myometrial smooth muscle cell, annotated with myometrial cell (CL:0002366). CL:0002366 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Downstream
Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening The FH-deficient route converges on the same fibrotic output as the transcriptional drivers, producing a tumor with the characteristic distorted staghorn vasculature and alveolar-pattern oedema.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36981015 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"This increases the levels of intracellular fumarate which inhibits the activity of hypoxia-inducible factor (HIF) prolyl hydroxylases, contributing to the accumulation of HIF-"
The mechanism of this node: fumarate inhibits the prolyl hydroxylases, so HIF-alpha accumulates without hypoxia.
Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening
The defining feature of a fibroid is not its cell count but its matrix: the mutated smooth muscle cells and the adjacent tumor-associated fibroblasts deposit disproportionate quantities of collagen, fibronectin and proteoglycans, which constitute a substantial fraction of tumor volume. The accumulated matrix is not inert. It stiffens the tissue, and it binds and prolongs the activity of the growth factors (TGF-beta chief among them) that drove its own deposition, so the matrix becomes a self-reinforcing feed-forward arm of the mechanism rather than the end of it. Matrix metalloproteinase activity is dysregulated in parallel, so remodeling as well as deposition is abnormal. This is the node on which the disease's name rests and the one that distinguishes a fibroid from a simple hyperplasia.

Animal models
Eker rat (germline Tsc2 mutation) Rat rescues The Eker rat is the system in which vitamin D3 was shown to restore the MRN complex/ATM DNA-repair axis in myometrial stem cells after developmental DES exposure, which is the experimental basis for the vitamin D environmental record in this entry.
Human leiomyoma tissue xenograft in immunodeficient mouse Mouse recapitulates The graft retains the histopathological morphology of the source tumor, matrix included, which is exactly the property that makes it the translational bridge.
Downstream
Tumor Growth and Clinical Manifestations Sustained proliferation plus excessive matrix deposition produce a growing, space-occupying smooth muscle tumor that distorts the uterine architecture and generates the clinical phenotype.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:36982852 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Excessive ECM accumulation and aberrant remodeling are crucial for fibrotic diseases and excessive ECM deposition is the central characteristics of UFs."
Names excessive ECM deposition and aberrant remodeling as the central characteristic of uterine fibroids, which is exactly what this node asserts.
PMID:36982852 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"An increased stiffness characterizes the fibrotic microenvironment, and this rigidity is associated with disease progression."
Supports the tissue-stiffening half of this node and its link to progression, which is why the node is tagged at the TISSUE scale rather than the cellular one.
PMID:41514933 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"They are benign tumors consisting of disorganized myofibroblasts embedded within significant amounts of extracellular matrix, which constitute a considerable portion of the tumor volume"
Quantifies the matrix as a considerable portion of tumor volume, supporting the claim that the matrix rather than the cell mass is the defining component.
Aberrant Pseudocapsule Angiogenesis and Venous Ectasia
Fibroid vasculature is organized differently from that of a malignant solid tumor. The fibroid mass itself is poorly vascularized and is instead supplied by a highly vascularized pseudocapsule that develops in response to its growth and appears as a peripheral "ring of fire" on Doppler ultrasound. The angiogenic drive is attributed principally to the insulin-like growth factor system rather than to the HIF-1-alpha/VEGF axis of hypoxic tumors, and HIF-1-alpha has been reported absent from fibroids where it is readily demonstrable in leiomyosarcoma - which is what establishes that the common fibroid does not use the hypoxia route, and is not contradicted by the pseudohypoxia node, whose HIF stabilization is metabolite-driven and confined to the uncommon FH-deficient subtype. Separately, the growing mass raises interstitial pressure and obstructs venous drainage, enlarging the endometrial and myometrial venous lakes. Both the structurally deficient pseudocapsule vessels and the dilated venous lakes are proposed sources of the heavy bleeding, and which of them dominates is unsettled. One correction the source makes about its own history is worth keeping: the interstitial-pressure explanation for the enlarged venous lakes was later challenged, with growth factors rather than mechanical pressure held largely responsible for enlarging the veins. The pressure account is recorded here because the microradiographic observation of enlarged lakes stands; its mechanical explanation does not.
endothelial cell CL:0000115 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves endothelial cell (CL:0000115). CL:0000115 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
angiogenesis GO:0001525 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased angiogenesis (GO:0001525). GO:0001525 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↑ INCREASED
Downstream
Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure Vessels of increased diameter, whether pseudocapsule neovessels or dilated venous lakes, are the substrate that the endometrial hemostatic mechanism then fails to close.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These also lead to downstream expression of angiogenic factors including IGF-1 and IGF-2, as opposed to the VEGF-driven mechanism found in the angiogenesis of hypoxic tumors. The resulting vasculature supplying the fibroid with nutrients and oxygen is highly irregular."
Establishes both halves of this node's angiogenic claim: the IGF-driven rather than VEGF-driven route, and the irregular vasculature that results.
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Microradiographic studies in the 1970's supported the notion that HMB resulted not from the fibroid vasculature itself but rather from venous lakes dilated and enlarged by virtue of increased interstitial pressure from the growing tumors"
Supports the venous-ectasia arm of this node, in which the mass effect of the tumor rather than its own vessels enlarges the draining veins.
Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure
Fibroids do not bleed only by mechanics. Fibroid tissue secretes TGF-beta-3 in response to ovarian steroids, and TGF-beta-3 acts on the overlying endometrium to downregulate BMP receptor 2 (which the source says is likely to cause defective decidualization) and to reduce expression of plasminogen activator inhibitor 1, antithrombin III and thrombomodulin, which the source says likely contributes to the bleeding. Both steps are hedged in the source and are hedged here. Note also that two of those three regulators are anticoagulants, so the net direction is an interpretation of the hemostatic outcome the source draws rather than a reading of each protein in isolation - the DECREASED modifier on the blood-coagulation process below records that net outcome, not a per-protein reading. The endometrium is therefore both less able to decidualize and less able to form and hold a hemostatic plug. This node is where the reproductive and the bleeding phenotypes share a mechanism. Scope note: the purely physical contributions to the bleeding - increased endometrial surface area, disordered myometrial contractility, and venous ectasia from compression - are real and are cited below, but they are properties of the tumor mass and belong to the Tumor Growth and Bulk Compression nodes. This node is the endometrial signalling arm, which is why it is tagged TISSUE.
endometrial stromal cell CL:0002255 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves endometrial stromal cell, annotated with stromal cell of endometrium (CL:0002255). CL:0002255 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
endometrial hemostatic plug formation GO:0007596 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased endometrial hemostatic plug formation, annotated with blood coagulation (GO:0007596). GO:0007596 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED endometrial decidualization GO:0046697 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased endometrial decidualization, annotated with decidualization (GO:0046697). GO:0046697 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Downstream
Heavy Menstrual Bleeding The hemostatic and vascular failure described by this node is what makes the menstrual loss heavy.
Infertility and Reproductive Morbidity Defective decidualization is the shared step by which the same TGF-beta-3 signal that impairs hemostasis also impairs implantation.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"TGF-β3 secreted by fibroids has been shown to induce BMP-2 resistance in endometrium by down-regulation of BMPR-2, likely causing defective endometrial decidualization"
Supports the decidualization arm: fibroid-derived TGF-beta-3 downregulates BMPR-2 in the endometrium and impairs decidualization.
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"TGF-β3 also reduces expression of plasminogen activator inhibitor-1 (PAI-1), Antithrombin III (ATIII), and thrombomodulin in endometrium, likely contributing to bleeding"
Supports the hemostatic arm: the same fibroid-derived TGF-beta-3 lowers three endometrial coagulation regulators, which the source links to the bleeding.
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Fibroids may cause HMB merely physically, i.e., through an increase in endometrial surface due to underlying fibroid growth; through an influence on normal myometrial contractility patterns; through an ulcerated or degenerating fibroid, or through uterine venous ectasia due to compression from..."
Enumerates the mechanical contributions this node records alongside the TGF-beta-3-mediated ones, and is the reason the node is not described as purely biochemical.
Tumor Growth and Clinical Manifestations
Progressive growth of the leiomyoma produces a benign smooth muscle tumor of the uterus. Submucosal and intramural tumors distort the endometrial cavity and impair hemostasis, causing heavy menstrual bleeding and consequent iron deficiency anemia. Bulk from larger tumors compresses adjacent pelvic organs (bladder, rectum), causing pressure symptoms, while distortion of the cavity and tubal ostia contributes to reproductive morbidity. Most affected women are asymptomatic: fibroids cause clinically significant disease in roughly a quarter of reproductive-age women overall, and affect quality of life in approximately 30% of cases.
leiomyoma smooth muscle cell CL:0000192 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves leiomyoma smooth muscle cell, annotated with smooth muscle cell (CL:0000192). CL:0000192 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Downstream
Bulk Compression of Adjacent Pelvic Organs Once the tumor is large enough, its mass alone displaces the neighbouring bladder and rectum; this arm of the mechanism is purely mechanical and is independent of the bleeding arm.
Pelvic Mass A tumor large enough to enlarge the uterus becomes palpable.
Pelvic Pain Bulk and pressure from the growing tumor produce pelvic pain and discomfort.
Dysmenorrhea Disordered myometrial contractility around the tumor makes menstruation painful.
Dyspareunia The enlarged, deformed uterus makes intercourse painful, position-dependently.
Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure A cavity-distorting tumor is the precondition for the endometrial signalling and hemostatic changes that produce the bleeding.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40214304 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Uterine fibroids (leiomyomas), the most common tumors in women and those assigned female at birth, originate from myometrial smooth muscle cells and cause heavy menstrual bleeding, anemia, pelvic discomfort, pregnancy loss, and obstruction of labor in approximately a quarter of reproductive-age women."
Connects the smooth muscle tumor to its core clinical manifestations: heavy menstrual bleeding, anemia, pelvic discomfort, pregnancy loss, and obstruction of labor.
PMID:38723935 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"in approximately 30% of cases, UFs affect the quality of life and women's health, with abnormal uterine bleeding and heavy menstrual bleeding being the most common complaints, along with iron deficiency (ID) and ID anemia."
Quantifies the symptomatic fraction and identifies AUB/HMB and iron deficiency anemia as the dominant complaints.
Bulk Compression of Adjacent Pelvic Organs
A fibroid large enough to enlarge and deform the uterus displaces the structures around it. Anterior and fundal tumors press on the bladder, producing urinary frequency and, when the ureters are involved, hydronephrosis; posterior tumors press on the rectum, producing constipation; a large fibroid can go further and cause urinary obstruction or, rarely, hydronephrosis. This is the one arm of the mechanism that is purely a consequence of volume and position: severity correlates with fibroid size, number and anatomical placement rather than with driver genotype, and the treatments that shrink or remove the tumor relieve it while the treatments that only control bleeding do not.
Downstream
Urinary Frequency Anterior and fundal tumors press on the bladder, reducing its capacity.
Constipation from Bulk Compression Posterior tumors press on the rectum.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The bulk growth of this extremely dense tissue leads an enlarged and deformed uterus and to some of the key symptoms associated with uterine fibroids in addition to HMB such as pressure symptoms, abdominal pain, and infertility"
Attributes the pressure symptoms specifically to the bulk of the dense tumor enlarging and deforming the uterus, which is the mechanical claim of this node.
PMID:40970558 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Fibroids positioned anteriorly or posteriorly can compress the bladder or rectum, resulting in urinary frequency, difficulty emptying the bladder, or constipation."
States the compression mechanism and both of its outputs directly, which is the whole content of this node.
PMID:40970558 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In severe cases, particularly large fibroids may lead to urinary obstruction or rare hydronephrosis"
Supplies the severe end of the compression spectrum named in this node's description, including the hydronephrosis that would otherwise be uncited.

Histopathology

3
Whorled fascicles of spindle smooth muscle cells in collagenous stroma
The conventional leiomyoma is a well-circumscribed nodule of bland spindle smooth muscle cells arranged in interlacing whorled fascicles, separated from the surrounding myometrium by a cleft, and set in an abundant collagen-rich stroma that accounts for much of the tumor's volume. Histology is confirmatory rather than diagnostic in routine practice, because fibroids are diagnosed on imaging and tissue is normally examined only after resection.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Despite the name, fibroids largely comprise of myocytes rather than fibroblasts and are characterized by the excessive deposition of extracellular matrix substances, mainly collagen, within the tumor"
Establishes the two components of this finding: a tumor made of smooth muscle cells rather than fibroblasts, in an excess of mainly collagenous matrix.
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"it is separated from the myometrium by a clear cleft, as observed in histological images"
Supports the circumscription and the cleft that separate the nodule from the adjacent myometrium on histology.
FH-deficient histologic signature VERY_FREQUENT
A recognizable constellation rather than a single feature: staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern oedema, bizarre nuclei, oval nuclei arranged in chains, prominent eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes, and eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules. More than four of these were present in 98.5% of an 80-case series. Confirmation is immunohistochemical: loss of FH staining (97.3% sensitive) or gain of S-(2-succino)-cysteine staining (100% in the five cases tested). Finding this pattern is what prompts germline FH testing, so it is the histology with the largest downstream consequence in this disease.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37263921 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"More than four histologic features, including staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern edema, bizarre nuclei, oval nuclei arranged in chains, prominent eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes and eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules were observed in 98.5% (67/68) patients."
Lists the exact constellation and its frequency within the FH-deficient series.
PMID:37263921 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"FH immunohistochemistry were performed in 74 cases; S-(2-succino)-cysteine (2SC) were also detected by immunohistochemistry in five cases."
Documents the immunohistochemical confirmation step, and the small number of 2SC cases behind its reported 100% sensitivity.
Cellular leiomyoma variant
A histologic variant with markedly increased cellularity and correspondingly less stroma, which raises the differential with endometrial stromal tumors and leiomyosarcoma. Molecularly it is enriched for HMGA2 aberration, then chromosome 1p deletion, then MED12 mutation, so the variant is not just a morphologic curiosity but tracks a different driver distribution from conventional leiomyoma.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34626221 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"36.5% showed HMGA2 overexpression at the IHC level, associated with increased mRNA expression in 14/14 cases."
Supports the enrichment of HMGA2 aberration in this histologic variant.

The same meta-analysis's adjusted analysis does not retain miscarriage, so this sentence contradicts the unadjusted association above. Recorded as a separate REFUTE item rather than folded into the first, because one evidence item cannot carry two opposite directions.

Medical Actions

14
GnRH Agonist Therapy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: leuprolide CHEBI:6427 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses leuprolide (CHEBI:6427). CHEBI:6427 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Peptide
GnRH agonists (e.g., leuprolide) cause pituitary downregulation and hypoestrogenism, producing temporary fibroid shrinkage and bleeding control. Used as a preoperative bridge; limited by menopausal symptoms, bone loss, and rebound growth after stopping.

Oral GnRH Antagonist Therapy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: elagolix NCIT:C153373 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses elagolix (NCIT:C153373). NCIT:C153373 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus. relugolix NCIT:C114498 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses relugolix (NCIT:C114498). NCIT:C114498 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus. linzagolix NCIT:C148479 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses linzagolix (NCIT:C148479). NCIT:C148479 is a therapeutic agent from the NCI Thesaurus.
Platform: Small molecule
Oral GnRH antagonists (elagolix, relugolix, linzagolix) directly block pituitary GnRH receptors to suppress ovarian steroid production, reducing heavy menstrual bleeding in a uterus-sparing, reversible regimen, usually with steroid add-back to avoid the hypoestrogenic effects that limit GnRH agonists. Linzagolix is the one agent with a lower-dose arm usable without mandatory add-back. The effect is suppressive, not curative: symptoms recur relatively quickly after the drug is stopped, which is the argument the PRIMROSE withdrawal study makes for long-term continuation.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:38723935 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"hormonal therapies, including combined oral contraceptives, gonadotropin-releasing hormone agonists or antagonists"
Documents GnRH antagonists among hormonal therapies for fibroid-related AUB.
PMID:33596357 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A total of 73% of the participants in the relugolix combination therapy group in trial L1 and 71% of those in trial L2 had a response (primary end point), as compared with 19% and 15%, respectively, of those in the placebo groups (P<0.001 for both comparisons)."
The LIBERTY 1 and 2 phase-3 result quantifying the bleeding response to relugolix plus oestradiol and norethindrone against placebo, which is the trial evidence behind this treatment class.
PMID:33596357 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Treatment with the combination of relugolix (an oral gonadotropin-releasing hormone-receptor antagonist), estradiol, and norethindrone acetate, administered once daily, may have efficacy in women with uterine fibroids and heavy bleeding while avoiding hypoestrogenic effects."
States the rationale for the add-back component this treatment record describes: the steroid add-back is what makes long-term GnRH-receptor blockade tolerable.

Mifepristone
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: mifepristone CHEBI:50692 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses mifepristone (CHEBI:50692). CHEBI:50692 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Antiprogestin studied for symptomatic fibroids, reducing abnormal uterine bleeding and fibroid-related dysmenorrhea. It acts on the same progesterone-receptor axis as the selective progesterone receptor modulators curated above, and is grouped with them mechanistically rather than with the symptomatic agents; its safety profile is described as more favourable than ulipristal acetate's but less well established.
Mechanism Target:
Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation — Blocking the progesterone receptor removes the paracrine WNT and growth-factor signal that this node depends on, which is the mechanism the bleeding and dysmenorrhea response is attributed to.
Show evidence (1 reference)

"mifepristone demonstrated its efficacy in reducing abnormal uterine bleedings (AUBs) and fibroid-related dysmenorrhea"
Documents mifepristone's efficacy for both of the symptoms this treatment record claims, in a review of the fibroid treatment pathway.

Environmental Factors

5
Chronic low vitamin D status
vitamin D deficiency Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is vitamin D deficiency.
Vitamin D deficiency is repeatedly associated with fibroid presence and size, and unlike most epidemiological risk factors it has a specific proposed mechanism at the initiating node rather than at the growth node: calcitriol acting through VDR supports the MRN-complex/ATM DNA-repair axis in myometrial stem cells, so deficiency plausibly permits the driver lesion to be acquired and retained. In vitro work shows vitamin D inhibiting fibroid growth through proliferation, ECM remodelling, DNA repair and apoptosis pathways; clinical studies support this only in part.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:33670322 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In recent years, epidemiological studies reported a vitamin D deficiency in patients with UFs raised interest in the potential biological effects of vitamin D supplementation."
Establishes the epidemiological association between vitamin D deficiency and uterine fibroids that this exposure record is about.
PMID:33670322 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In vitro studies proved vitamin D efficacy in inhibiting UFs growth by targeting pathways involved in the regulation of various biological processes, including proliferation, extracellular matrix (ECM) remodeling, DNA repair, signaling and apoptosis."
Supports the mechanistic half of the claim, naming the DNA-repair and ECM pathways this record links to the pathograph.
PMID:33670322 REFUTE Human Clinical
"However, clinical studies supported only in part the beneficial effects of vitamin D supplementation in reducing UFs growth and tumor volume."
Contradicts the stronger reading that supplementation is an established fibroid-directed treatment. Kept as a separate REFUTE item because the same source supports the association and undercuts the therapeutic claim.
Mechanism Target:
PREDISPOSES Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation — Low vitamin D status is linked to the initiating lesion rather than to hormonal growth: VDR signalling supports the MRN-complex/ATM DNA-damage response in myometrial stem cells, and its loss leaves DNA damage unrepaired in exactly the cell population from which fibroids arise.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32545544 SUPPORT Model Organism
"VitD3 acts on DNA repair via the MRN complex/ATM axis, restores the DNA repair signaling network, and enhances DDR."
Names the repair axis vitamin D acts through in myometrial stem cells, which is the intermediate this edge asserts between vitamin D status and the driver lesion.
Developmental exposure to endocrine-disrupting chemicals
exposure to xenoestrogen ECTO:9002036 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is exposure to xenoestrogen (ECTO:9002036). ECTO:9002036 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Early-life exposure of the myometrium to endocrine-disrupting chemicals raises adult fibroid risk. The strongest mechanistic evidence is from Eker rats exposed neonatally to diethylstilbestrol, whose myometrial stem cells show reduced RAD50 and MRE11 and a defective DNA damage response at the pro-fibroid stage, months before tumors appear. In humans the corresponding exposures reported are xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, diethylstilbestrol, and phthalates and bisphenol A.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:36982852 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These factors include race, age, parity, family history, food additives, obesity, vitamin D deficiency, and endocrine-disrupting chemical exposure"
Human review listing endocrine-disrupting chemical exposure among the established fibroid risk factors. Cited here rather than the opening sentence of the Eker-rat paper, which asserts the same association but is a rodent study and so cannot carry a HUMAN_CLINICAL grade.
PMID:32545544 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Results showed that there was a significantly reduced expression of RAD50 and MRE11, key DNA repair proteins in DES-exposed myometrial tissues, compared to vehicle (VEH)-exposed tissues (p < 0.01)."
The rat experimental result behind the mechanism: developmental DES exposure depletes two MRN-complex repair proteins in myometrium.
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The risk factors include advanced reproductive age, African and Latin American ethnicity, UL cases in family medical history, obesity, early menarche, arterial hypertension, chronic inflammation, exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, sexually transmitted infections and alcohol"
Independent review listing exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis among the established risk factors, which is the exposure term bound here.
Mechanism Target:
PREDISPOSES Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation — Developmental xenoestrogen exposure leaves the myometrial stem cell pool repair-deficient, which is how an exposure decades earlier raises the probability of the somatic driver lesion that starts a fibroid.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32545544 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Results showed that there was a significantly reduced expression of RAD50 and MRE11, key DNA repair proteins in DES-exposed myometrial tissues, compared to vehicle (VEH)-exposed tissues (p < 0.01)."
Supplies the intermediate step - repair-protein loss in the exposed myometrium - that links the developmental exposure to the initiating lesion.
Obesity and excess adiposity
obesity and high body mass index Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is obesity and high body mass index.
Obesity is one of the most consistently reported fibroid risk factors, and the proposed mechanism is specific: adipose tissue performs the same aromatase and 17-beta-hydroxysteroid-dehydrogenase conversion of androgens to oestrogens that fibroid cells themselves use, so excess adiposity raises the oestrogen supply to a tumor that depends on it. This is why the edge below attaches to the hormone-driven proliferation node rather than to the initiating lesion.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The risk factors include advanced reproductive age, African and Latin American ethnicity, UL cases in family medical history, obesity, early menarche, arterial hypertension, chronic inflammation, exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, sexually transmitted infections and alcohol"
Lists obesity among the established risk factors for uterine leiomyoma.
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The enzymatic conversion of androgens to oestrogens is also typical of fat tissue"
Gives the mechanism this record attributes the obesity association to, and is the reason the pathograph edge targets the hormone node.
Mechanism Target:
PREDISPOSES Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation — Adipose aromatase activity adds to the circulating oestrogen pool that the established fibroid clone depends on for growth.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The enzymatic conversion of androgens to oestrogens is also typical of fat tissue"
Names the aromatization step by which adiposity feeds the oestrogen-dependent growth node.
Alcohol consumption
exposure to alcohol consumption ECTO:0001082 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is exposure to alcohol consumption (ECTO:0001082). ECTO:0001082 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Alcohol appears in the risk-factor lists of contemporary reviews of uterine leiomyoma genesis. No mechanism specific to fibroids is established for it, so it is recorded as an association and its pathograph edge is left off rather than attached to a node on speculation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The risk factors include advanced reproductive age, African and Latin American ethnicity, UL cases in family medical history, obesity, early menarche, arterial hypertension, chronic inflammation, exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, sexually transmitted infections and alcohol"
Lists alcohol last among the risk factors for uterine leiomyoma in this review.
Parity
parity Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is parity.
Having borne children is consistently reported as protective, in contrast to the nulliparity and early-menarche exposures that raise risk. Parity, oral contraceptive use and smoking are the three protective factors this review names; smoking is recorded there too but is not curated here as a protective exposure, because a protective association that nobody would act on is a poor fit for a slot that reads as an intervention.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Parity, oral contraceptive intake and smoking are among possible protective factors for UL development"
Names parity first among the possible protective factors for uterine leiomyoma.
Mechanism Target:
PROTECTS_AGAINST Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation — Parity interrupts the sequence of ovulatory cycles that supplies the oestrogen and progesterone this node depends on. The intermediates are not established, which is why the link type says so rather than naming a pathway.
🩻

Imaging Findings

3
Well-circumscribed hypoechoic myometrial mass on transvaginal ultrasound
Ultrasound is the first-line modality and is where the diagnosis is normally made: it establishes presence, size, number and location. Because examination is unreliable, imaging rather than palpation drives management, and reports are expected to place each fibroid in the FIGO 0-8 scheme describing its relationship to endometrium, myometrium and serosa.
Ultrasound Diagnostic
Uterine leiomyoma HP:0000131 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40970558 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Ultrasound should be the first-line diagnostic modality, and magnetic resonance imaging should be reserved for complex cases and/or surgical planning."
States the first-line role of ultrasound and the reserved role of MRI.
PMID:40970558 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Imaging reports should follow the FIGO classification system for uterine fibroids, to enable a standardized description of their relationship to the endometrium, myometrium, and uterine serosa."
Supports the FIGO reporting convention this finding is expected to be described in.
Peripheral "ring of fire" vascularity on Doppler
Colour Doppler shows flow concentrated at the rim of an intramural fibroid rather than through it, reflecting the vascularized pseudocapsule that supplies a tumor whose own mass is poorly vascularized. This is the imaging correlate of the pseudocapsule angiogenesis node.
Ultrasound
Uterine leiomyoma HP:0000131 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"This structure can be seen as a “ring of fire” in ultrasound Doppler imaging, and it is separated from the myometrium by a clear cleft"
Names the Doppler appearance and attributes it to the pseudocapsule.
Multiplanar MRI characterization for surgical planning
MRI is reserved for complex cases and surgical planning, where it distinguishes fibroids from adenomyosis and from malignancy, predicts therapeutic response, and maps the tumors for intervention. It does not, however, reliably exclude leiomyosarcoma before resection.
Mri Diagnostic
Uterine leiomyoma HP:0000131 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP)

Lists the four purposes this finding is curated for, including the differential against adenomyosis and malignancy.
📈

Progression

3
Premenarchal
Age: birth to menarche
Fibroids essentially do not arise before menarche, which follows from their growth being sex-steroid-dependent: the tumor, though initiated by a somatic lesion, cannot expand without ovarian steroid support, and before menarche that support is absent.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37668348 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"Uterine fibroids (UFs) are benign tumors arising from the uterus, characterized by accumulation of abundant extracellular matrix (ECM) and sex steroid-dependent growth."
Establishes that fibroid growth is sex-steroid-dependent. The premenarchal absence recorded in this phase follows from that dependence rather than being stated in the quote, which is why this is graded INDIRECT.
Reproductive years
Age: menarche to menopause, peak 35-50 years
The growth phase. Detection and symptom burden rise steeply from the mid-thirties, with most diagnoses between 35 and 50. Individual tumors within one uterus are separate clones and grow at different rates, so "progression" here is the aggregate of several independent trajectories rather than one.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36982825 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Despite its benign nature and low risk of malignisation, UL can be characterised by rapid growth, large size and multiple nodes"
Supports the variable-growth, multiple-tumor character of this phase.
Postmenopausal
Age: after menopause
Ovarian steroid withdrawal is followed by growth arrest and frequently by regression, which is the therapeutic logic behind every hormonal treatment in this entry: they reproduce the postmenopausal state pharmacologically, and their effect reverses when stopped for the same reason.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33670322 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Furthermore, UFs can undergo growth inhibition or spontaneous regression after menopause"
States the postmenopausal growth inhibition and regression that define this phase.

Prevalence

4
Women aged 30-44 years
Point Prevalence 60000.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
Uterine fibroids are the most common benign pelvic tumor of reproductive-age women, present in more than 60% of women aged 30-44 years (>60% = >60,000 per 100,000; recorded as a lower bound).
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38723935 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Uterine fibroids (UFs) are the most common female benign pelvic tumors, affecting >60% of patients aged 30-44 years."
Quantifies fibroid prevalence at over 60% in women aged 30-44 years.
Women overall (with racial disparity)
Point Prevalence 70000.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
Overall prevalence is approximately 70% (70,000 per 100,000), with Black women affected at a disproportionately high rate.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40862769 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Uterine fibroids are benign smooth muscle tumors that affect ~70% of women, with Black women being affected at a disproportionate rate."
Documents overall fibroid prevalence (~70%) and the disproportionate burden in Black women.
Global, GBD 2021, age-standardized
Point Prevalence 2841.07 per 100,000 (2164.43–3682.27) >1 in 1,000
GBD 2021 age-standardized prevalence rate, rising from 2799.88 per 100,000 in 1990. Note that this is an order of magnitude below the 60-70% screening-based figures recorded above and does not contradict them: GBD models diagnosed disease, while the screening studies count fibroids found on systematic ultrasound in women who mostly have no symptoms. The gap between the two is the asymptomatic fraction.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41048892 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The ASPR increased from 2799.88 (2133.46, 3650.54) to 2841.07 (2164.43, 3682.27) [EAPC 0.04 (0.03, 0.06)]."
Gives the age-standardized prevalence rate and its uncertainty interval for 2021, which are the values recorded here.
Global, GBD 2021, age-standardized
Annual Incidence 250.93 per 100,000 (183.44–330.94) per year >1 in 1,000 per year
GBD 2021 age-standardized incidence rate, up from 234.36 per 100,000 in 1990. rate_denominator is set explicitly because an incidence measure has no fallback: this is new cases per 100,000 population per year. No qualitative prevalence tier is used here, since those presuppose a prevalence rather than an incidence measure.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41048892 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Between 1990 and 2021, the global ASIR of UF rose from 234.36 (95%UI: 171.06, 309.92) to 250.93 (183.44, 330.94) per 100,000 [EAPC 0.24 (0.23, 0.25)]."
Gives the age-standardized incidence rate and interval for 2021 recorded here, plus the direction of travel since 1990.
⚖️

Clinical Burden

Variable
The burden is genuinely bimodal rather than merely heterogeneous, and averaging it would misdescribe the disease. Most women with fibroids never know they have them; among those who do, roughly 30% report a quality-of-life impact, and for that minority heavy menstrual bleeding is the dominant complaint and the leading reason for elective hysterectomy. Mortality is essentially nil. So the same diagnosis covers an incidental ultrasound finding and an indication for major surgery.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38723935 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"in approximately 30% of cases, UFs affect the quality of life and women's health, with abnormal uterine bleeding and heavy menstrual bleeding being the most common complaints, along with iron deficiency (ID) and ID anemia."
Quantifies the symptomatic minority and names what dominates their burden.
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In ~30% of patients, uterine fibroids cause menorrhagia, or heavy menstrual bleeding, and more than half of the patients experience symptoms such as heavy menstrual bleeding, pelvic pain, or infertility."
Independent figure for the symptomatic fraction, supporting the VARIABLE band rather than a single level.
🔀

Differential Diagnoses

3

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Uterine Leiomyoma:

Uterine leiomyosarcoma Not Yet Curated MONDO:0016262
Overlapping Features The differential that matters, because it cannot be resolved before resection. No imaging finding or biomarker reliably separates a benign leiomyoma from a leiomyosarcoma preoperatively, which is why a rapidly enlarging mass prompts evaluation and why morcellation of a presumed fibroid carries a dissemination risk.
Distinguishing Features
  • Histopathology of the resected specimen is definitive; nothing before resection is.
  • Molecular profiling may assist in the cellular and variant morphologies.
  • HIF-1-alpha is readily demonstrable in leiomyosarcoma but has been reported absent from leiomyoma, which is also why the two tumors' angiogenic mechanisms differ.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36303616 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"HIF-1α for example has been shown lacking in uterine fibroids, when it was readily shown in leiomyosarcomata"
Supports the HIF-1-alpha distinction recorded under distinguishing_features.
Endometrial stromal tumor
Overlapping Features Endometrial stromal nodule and low-grade endometrial stromal sarcoma enter the differential chiefly against the cellular leiomyoma variant, where increased cellularity and reduced stroma remove the morphologic cues that separate them.
Distinguishing Features
  • Immunohistochemistry is suggestive rather than decisive: cellular leiomyomas express CD10 in a majority of cases (65.4%, though only 42.2% strongly, at H-score above 50) and IFITM1 in 36.5%, so positivity for an endometrial stromal marker does not settle it.
  • Smooth muscle markers are near-uniformly expressed in cellular leiomyoma (calponin and desmin in 100%), which is the more reliable direction of evidence.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34626221 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Concerning markers of endometrial stromal differentiation, the expression of CD10 was observed in 65.4% cases (42.2% with H-score > 50), and IFITM1 in 36.5% cases (1.9% with H-score > 50)"
Gives the immunohistochemical overlap that makes this differential hard: CD10 is positive in most cellular leiomyomas and IFITM1 in over a third, so positivity for an endometrial stromal marker does not settle the diagnosis.
PMID:34626221 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The immunohistochemical results showed a high expression of smooth muscle markers (calponin (100%), desmin (100%), smooth muscle actin (98.1%), caldesmon (96.1%), transgelin (96.1%), smooth muscle myosin heavy chain (86.5%), and smoothelin (61.5%))."
Supplies the smooth-muscle marker panel that argues for leiomyoma, and the reason it is described as the more reliable direction of evidence.
Adenomyosis MONDO:0010888
Overlapping Features Ectopic endometrial tissue within the myometrium, presenting with the same heavy bleeding and dysmenorrhea, frequently coexisting with fibroids, and separated from them on imaging rather than clinically.
Distinguishing Features
  • MRI is the modality that resolves the two, and standardized structured reporting exists precisely because this differential is common.
  • The two frequently coexist, so resolving it is not always a choice between them.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40970558 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Adherence to standardized structured reporting across imaging modalities is critical to improve diagnostic accuracy, differentiate fibroids from conditions such as adenomyosis or malignancies, predict therapeutic responses, and plan surgical interventions."
Names adenomyosis as one of the two conditions imaging is expected to distinguish fibroids from.

Animal Models

2
Eker rat (germline Tsc2 mutation) Spontaneous
The most widely used spontaneous model. Roughly 65% of female Eker rats develop uterine smooth muscle tumors at 12-16 months through a germline Tsc2 mutation and consequent mTOR pathway activation. Histomorphology and oestrogen/progesterone dependence closely resemble human fibroids, and the strain has carried most of the gene-environment work in this field, including the developmental DES exposure and vitamin D3 rescue experiments this entry cites.
Species
Rat
Genotype
Tsc2 germline loss-of-function (Eker strain)
Genes
Tsc2 hgnc:12363 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this experimental model concerns this gene This experimental model concerns Tsc2 (hgnc:12363). hgnc:12363 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Publication
PMID:41810407
Pathograph links
Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate Reproduces spontaneous, hormone-dependent uterine smooth muscle tumorigenesis in an intact animal, which is what makes it usable for gene-environment and chemoprevention work no in vitro system can do.
Limitations: The driving lesion is Tsc2, which is not one of the recurrent human somatic drivers (MED12, HMGA2, FH), so the model recapitulates the hormone-dependent growth phase without recapitulating the initiating genotype. It also takes 12-16 months, is expensive, and the animals develop concurrent renal and hepatic tumors that confound interpretation.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41810407 SUPPORT Model Organism
"This rat model exhibits highly similar histomorphology and estrogen/progesterone-dependent manifestations to human ULs"
Supports treating this model as informative for the hormone-dependent growth node, which is the claim this link makes.
+ 1 more reference
Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation fails to recapitulate fidelity: low The model does not reproduce the human initiating lesion. Its tumors start from a germline Tsc2 mutation present in every cell, where human fibroids start from a somatic driver acquired by one myometrial cell, so the monoclonal-origin and driver-genotype claims of this node are outside what the model can support.
Limitations: Tsc2 is not among MED12, HMGA2, FH or COL4A5/COL4A6, and the lesion is germline rather than somatic. Any inference from this model to the human initiating event is an inference about hormone-dependent growth in a predisposed myometrium, not about how a human fibroid starts.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41810407 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Eker rats (Rattus norvegicus), due to their germline mutation in the tuberous sclerosis complex type 2 (Tsc2) tumor suppressor gene, spontaneously develop uterine smooth muscle tumors in approximately 65% of females at 12 to 16 months of age."
Identifies the model's lesion as a germline Tsc2 mutation, which is what establishes the mismatch with the human somatic drivers this node is about.
Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening rescues fidelity: moderate The Eker rat is the system in which vitamin D3 was shown to restore the MRN complex/ATM DNA-repair axis in myometrial stem cells after developmental DES exposure, which is the experimental basis for the vitamin D environmental record in this entry.
Limitations: The measured endpoint is DNA damage and repair-protein expression in isolated myometrial stem cells, not matrix deposition or tumor volume, so the link to the fibrotic output node is an extrapolation across both scale and endpoint.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32545544 SUPPORT Model Organism
"This study demonstrates, for the first time, that VitD3 treatment attenuated the DNA damage load in MMSCs exposed to DES and classic DNA damage inducers."
The rescue result this link records, and the reason the divergences above are stated: the endpoint is DNA damage, not matrix.
The gene_term binds the human TSC2 ortholog because HGNC codes human genes; the model's own lesion is rat Tsc2. MGI/RGD identifiers for the rat gene are not bound by any current dismech slot.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41810407 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Because of the convenience of model establishment, this model has become one of the most commonly used models for ULs"
Establishes this strain's standing as the most commonly used uterine leiomyoma model, which is why it is curated here rather than only mentioned in notes.
Human leiomyoma tissue xenograft in immunodeficient mouse Xenograft
Human fibroid tissue grafted subcutaneously or beneath the renal capsule of immunodeficient mice, usually with a sustained-release hormone device to keep the graft in its native hormonal environment. This is the model that preserves the human tumor's own driver genotype, heterogeneity and microenvironment, and so the one used for preclinical efficacy work meant to translate.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID) host, human leiomyoma tissue graft
Publication
PMID:41810407
Pathograph links
Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening recapitulates fidelity: high The graft retains the histopathological morphology of the source tumor, matrix included, which is exactly the property that makes it the translational bridge.
Limitations: Requires an immunodeficient host, so the immune and inflammatory contributions to fibroid matrix biology cannot be studied in it at all. Results also vary between tissue donors, and the model is technically demanding and expensive.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41810407 SUPPORT Model Organism
"These models closely replicate the histopathological morphology of human ULs. However, their broader application remains limited by technical complexity, stringent experimental requirements, and high costs."
Supports both the HIGH fidelity for morphology and the practical limitations recorded on this link.

States the reason this model class is curated: it is the closest available reproduction of human fibroid pathology and the intended translational bridge.

click to show 
name: Uterine Leiomyoma
creation_date: "2026-06-22T00:00:00Z"
description: >-
  Uterine leiomyomas are benign monoclonal smooth muscle tumors that arise from a single myometrial cell acquiring a driver mutation in MED12, HMGA2, or FH.
  Ovarian estrogen and progesterone then drive smooth muscle proliferation and abundant extracellular matrix deposition during each ovulatory cycle.
  Progressive tumor growth distorts the uterus, producing abnormal uterine bleeding, bulk symptoms, and reproductive complications.
category: Complex
disease_term:
  preferred_term: uterine fibroid
  term:
    id: MONDO:0007886
    label: uterine corpus leiomyoma
parents:
  - Benign uterine neoplasm
  - Smooth muscle tumor

has_subtypes:
- name: MED12-mutant
  display_name: MED12-mutant leiomyoma
  description: >
    The dominant molecular subtype, defined by a recurrent somatic exon-2 point mutation
    in MED12 disrupting the CDK8/CDK19 kinase module of the Mediator complex. These
    tumors tend to be multiple rather than solitary, are found even at small size, are
    more frequent in Black women, and carry a distinctive ECM-organization and
    Wnt/beta-catenin transcriptional program. Reported frequency spans 50-80% of tumors
    depending on cohort and detection method.
  genes:
  - preferred_term: MED12
    term:
      id: hgnc:11957
      label: MED12
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:37668348
    reference_title: "RISING STARS: Role of MED12 mutation in the pathogenesis of uterine fibroids."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Among the driver gene mutations identified in UFs, mutations \nin MED12, a component of the cyclin-dependent kinase (CDK) Mediator module, are \nthe most common and observed in 50-80% of UFs."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes MED12-mutant tumors as the most common molecular subtype at 50-80%.

- name: HMGA2-rearranged
  display_name: HMGA2-rearranged / HMGA2-overexpressing leiomyoma
  description: >
    The second subtype, defined by 12q14-15 rearrangement or overexpression of the
    architectural transcription factor HMGA2. Around 10% of fibroids overall, but
    markedly enriched in the cellular leiomyoma histologic variant, where HMGA2
    overexpression was found in 36.5% and HMGA2 rearrangement in 13.2% of cases.
    Largely mutually exclusive with MED12 mutation.
  genes:

    term:
      id: hgnc:5009
      label: HMGA2

    explanation: >-
      Quantifies HMGA2 overexpression and rearrangement in the cellular leiomyoma
      variant where this subtype concentrates.
  - reference: PMID:38326302

    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-
      Supplies the citation for the mutual-exclusivity claim in this subtype's

- name: FH-deficient
  display_name: FH-deficient leiomyoma
  description: >
    An uncommon but clinically consequential subtype defined by biallelic loss of
    fumarate hydratase, recognizable on routine histology by a cluster of features
    (staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern oedema, bizarre nuclei, eosinophilic nucleoli
    with perinucleolar haloes, eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules) and confirmable by
    loss of FH or gain of 2SC staining. It matters out of proportion to its frequency
    because a large fraction of cases carry a germline FH variant - 31 of 80 tumors
    (39%) in the reference series, or 52% of those in which sequencing found a variant at
    all - which makes the patient and her relatives HLRCC kindred at risk of renal cell
    carcinoma.
  genes:
  - preferred_term: FH
    term:
      id: hgnc:3700
      label: FH
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:37263921

    snippet: "More than four histologic features, including staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern edema, bizarre nuclei, oval nuclei arranged in chains, prominent eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes and eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules were observed in 98.5% (67/68) patients."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the recognizable histologic signature that defines this subtype in
      practice, present in 98.5% of the series.

pathophysiology:
- name: Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation
  description: >
    Uterine leiomyomas (fibroids) originate as monoclonal tumors from a single
    myometrial smooth muscle cell (or a myometrial stem cell acquiring tumor-initiating
    features) that acquires a recurrent somatic driver mutation. The most common driver
    is a MED12 exon-2 mutation (~70-77%), followed by HMGA2/HMGA1 overexpression or
    chromosomal rearrangement (~10%, 12q14-15), biallelic FH (fumarate hydratase) loss
    defining the FH-deficient subtype (also seen in hereditary leiomyomatosis and renal
    cell cancer, HLRCC), with rarer collagen IV (COL4A5/COL4A6) deletions also reported. Heterozygous MED12 mutations
    disrupt the CDK8/CDK19 kinase module of the Mediator complex, altering the chromatin
    landscape, enhancer engagement, genomic stability, and progesterone responsiveness.

    Scoping decision, recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight: COL4A5/COL4A6
    deletion and the SRCAP-complex class are named here but are deliberately not given
    has_subtypes entries. Both are recurrent but rare relative to the three modelled
    classes, neither has a distinct pathophysiology arm this entry could populate beyond
    the shared ECM output, and neither changes management. A curator with a
    collagen-IV-specific mechanism or frequency source should add the subtype then.
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  genetic_context:
    variant_origin: SOMATIC
    functional_impact_category: GAIN_OF_FUNCTION
    notes: >-
      This block describes the MED12 arm only, which is the majority route and the one
      the node's evidence characterizes. It does not describe FH, whose lesion is
      biallelic loss of a tumor suppressor (LOSS_OF_FUNCTION) and is germline in roughly
      half of FH-deficient tumors - see the separate FH node and the FH-deficient
      subtype. It does not describe HMGA2 rearrangement either, which is a structural
      event rather than a point-mutation gain of function. The slots are single-valued,
      so restricting them to one arm and saying so is more accurate than picking a value
      that is wrong for two of the three genes.

- name: Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation
  conforms_to: "sex_steroid_driven_proliferation#Target-Cell Population Expansion"
  description: >
    Estradiol and progesterone are essential for fibroid growth. During each ovulatory
    cycle the myometrium proliferates under ovarian steroid stimulation. Progesterone,
    acting through its nuclear receptor (PGR) in differentiated fibroid cells, activates
    paracrine secretion of WNT ligands, cytokines, and growth factors that drive a small
    stem cell population to proliferate. Fibroids rarely arise before menarche and
    frequently regress after menopause, underscoring steroid hormone dependence. The
    excessive matrix these proliferating cells and the adjacent tumor-associated
    fibroblasts lay down is modelled as the separate downstream node
    "Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening"; this node is the
    cell-population expansion alone.

- name: Fumarate Accumulation in FH-Deficient Leiomyoma
  conforms_to: "oncometabolite_dioxygenase_inhibition#Oncometabolite Accumulation"
  description: >
    In the fumarate-hydratase-deficient subtype, biallelic FH loss blocks the
    tricarboxylic-acid cycle at the fumarate-to-malate step, so fumarate accumulates and
    non-enzymatically succinates protein cysteine residues, forming the
    S-(2-succino)-cysteine adduct that immunohistochemistry detects. This node is the
    accumulation step alone; what the accumulated fumarate then does is the downstream
    pseudohypoxia node, matching the way the module it conforms to separates the two.
    The subtype is defined by the same chemistry whether the first FH hit is germline or
    somatic, and the germline case is hereditary leiomyomatosis and renal cell cancer,
    curated separately as Hereditary_Leiomyomatosis_and_Renal_Cell_Cancer.
