- name: Pseudohypoxic HIF Stabilization in FH-Deficient Leiomyoma
  conforms_to: "oncometabolite_dioxygenase_inhibition#Pseudohypoxic HIF Stabilization"
  description: >
    With the HIF prolyl hydroxylases inhibited by fumarate, HIF-alpha escapes degradation
    and a hypoxia transcriptional programme runs in a normoxic cell. This is the FH
    subtype's distinctive route to the tumor's distorted, staghorn vasculature.

    Read this alongside the angiogenesis node, which records HIF-1-alpha as absent from
    uterine fibroids. The two are not in conflict but the boundary matters: that
    observation comes from unselected fibroids, which are overwhelmingly MED12- or
    HMGA2-driven, and it is what establishes that the common fibroid does NOT use the
    hypoxia/VEGF route. Pseudohypoxia is specific to the uncommon FH-deficient subtype,
    where it is driven by a metabolite rather than by low oxygen. A reader who took the
    pseudohypoxia claim to apply to fibroids generally would have it backwards.

    explanation: >-

- name: Excessive Extracellular Matrix Deposition and Tissue Stiffening
  conforms_to: "fibrotic_response#Excessive ECM Deposition"
  description: >
    The defining feature of a fibroid is not its cell count but its matrix: the mutated
    smooth muscle cells and the adjacent tumor-associated fibroblasts deposit
    disproportionate quantities of collagen, fibronectin and proteoglycans, which
    constitute a substantial fraction of tumor volume. The accumulated matrix is not
    inert. It stiffens the tissue, and it binds and prolongs the activity of the growth
    factors (TGF-beta chief among them) that drove its own deposition, so the matrix
    becomes a self-reinforcing feed-forward arm of the mechanism rather than the end of
    it. Matrix metalloproteinase activity is dysregulated in parallel, so remodeling as
    well as deposition is abnormal. This is the node on which the disease's name rests
    and the one that distinguishes a fibroid from a simple hyperplasia.
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: leiomyoma smooth muscle cell
    term:
      id: CL:0000192
      label: smooth muscle cell
  - preferred_term: tumor-associated fibroblast
    term:
      id: CL:0000057
      label: fibroblast
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: excessive extracellular matrix deposition
    term:
      id: GO:0030198
      label: extracellular matrix organization
    modifier: INCREASED
  - preferred_term: collagen fibril organization
    term:
      id: GO:0030199
      label: collagen fibril organization
    modifier: INCREASED
  - preferred_term: TGF-beta receptor signaling
    term:
      id: GO:0007179
      label: transforming growth factor beta receptor signaling pathway
    modifier: INCREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36982852
    reference_title: Update on the Role and Regulatory Mechanism of Extracellular Matrix in the Pathogenesis of Uterine Fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-
      Names excessive ECM deposition and aberrant remodeling as the central
      characteristic of uterine fibroids, which is exactly what this node asserts.
  - reference: PMID:36982852
    reference_title: Update on the Role and Regulatory Mechanism of Extracellular Matrix in the Pathogenesis of Uterine Fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "An increased stiffness characterizes the fibrotic microenvironment, and this rigidity is associated with disease progression."
    explanation: >-
      Supports the tissue-stiffening half of this node and its link to progression,
      which is why the node is tagged at the TISSUE scale rather than the cellular one.
  - reference: PMID:41514933
    reference_title: "Beyond VEGF and TGF-β: A Comprehensive Review of Growth Factor Pathways in the Pathophysiology of Uterine Leiomyomas."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "They are benign tumors consisting of disorganized myofibroblasts embedded within significant amounts of extracellular matrix, which constitute a considerable portion of the tumor volume"
    explanation: >-
      Quantifies the matrix as a considerable portion of tumor volume, supporting the
      claim that the matrix rather than the cell mass is the defining component.
  downstream:
  - target: Tumor Growth and Clinical Manifestations
    description: >-
      Sustained proliferation plus excessive matrix deposition produce a growing,
      space-occupying smooth muscle tumor that distorts the uterine architecture and
      generates the clinical phenotype.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:36303616
      reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "The bulk growth of this extremely dense tissue leads an enlarged and deformed uterus and to some of the key symptoms associated with uterine fibroids in addition to HMB such as pressure symptoms, abdominal pain, and infertility"
      explanation: >-
        Draws the step this edge asserts: the accumulated dense matrix is what makes the
        tumor a space-occupying lesion that deforms the uterus.

- name: Aberrant Pseudocapsule Angiogenesis and Venous Ectasia
  description: >
    Fibroid vasculature is organized differently from that of a malignant solid tumor.
    The fibroid mass itself is poorly vascularized and is instead supplied by a highly
    vascularized pseudocapsule that develops in response to its growth and appears as a
    peripheral "ring of fire" on Doppler ultrasound. The angiogenic drive is attributed
    principally to the insulin-like growth factor system rather than to the
    HIF-1-alpha/VEGF axis of hypoxic tumors, and HIF-1-alpha has been reported absent
    from fibroids where it is readily demonstrable in leiomyosarcoma - which is what
    establishes that the common fibroid does not use the hypoxia route, and is not
    contradicted by the pseudohypoxia node, whose HIF stabilization is metabolite-driven
    and confined to the uncommon FH-deficient subtype. Separately, the
    growing mass raises interstitial pressure and obstructs venous drainage, enlarging
    the endometrial and myometrial venous lakes. Both the structurally deficient
    pseudocapsule vessels and the dilated venous lakes are proposed sources of the
    heavy bleeding, and which of them dominates is unsettled.

    One correction the source makes about its own history is worth keeping: the
    interstitial-pressure explanation for the enlarged venous lakes was later challenged,
    with growth factors rather than mechanical pressure held largely responsible for
    enlarging the veins. The pressure account is recorded here because the
    microradiographic observation of enlarged lakes stands; its mechanical explanation
    does not.
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: endothelial cell
    term:
      id: CL:0000115
      label: endothelial cell
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: angiogenesis
    term:
      id: GO:0001525
      label: angiogenesis
    modifier: INCREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "These also lead to downstream expression of angiogenic factors including IGF-1 and IGF-2, as opposed to the VEGF-driven mechanism found in the angiogenesis of hypoxic tumors. The resulting vasculature supplying the fibroid with nutrients and oxygen is highly irregular."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes both halves of this node's angiogenic claim: the IGF-driven rather
      than VEGF-driven route, and the irregular vasculature that results.
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Microradiographic studies in the 1970's supported the notion that HMB resulted not from the fibroid vasculature itself but rather from venous lakes dilated and enlarged by virtue of increased interstitial pressure from the growing tumors"
    explanation: >-
      Supports the venous-ectasia arm of this node, in which the mass effect of the
      tumor rather than its own vessels enlarges the draining veins.
  downstream:
  - target: Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure
    description: >-
      Vessels of increased diameter, whether pseudocapsule neovessels or dilated venous
      lakes, are the substrate that the endometrial hemostatic mechanism then fails to
      close.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:36303616
      reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "the heavy bleeding resulted from a failure of the fibrin/platelet plugs formed in the coagulation cascade in closing these blood vessels of increased diameter successfully"
      explanation: >-
        States the causal step this edge draws: enlarged vessels plus failed fibrin
        and platelet plugs produce the heavy bleeding.

- name: Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure
  description: >
    Fibroids do not bleed only by mechanics. Fibroid tissue secretes TGF-beta-3 in
    response to ovarian steroids, and TGF-beta-3 acts on the overlying endometrium to
    downregulate BMP receptor 2 (which the source says is likely to cause defective
    decidualization) and to reduce expression of plasminogen activator inhibitor 1,
    antithrombin III and thrombomodulin, which the source says likely contributes to the
    bleeding. Both steps are hedged in the source and are hedged here. Note also that two
    of those three regulators are anticoagulants, so the net direction is an
    interpretation of the hemostatic outcome the source draws rather than a reading of
    each protein in isolation - the DECREASED modifier on the blood-coagulation process
    below records that net outcome, not a per-protein reading. The endometrium is
    therefore both less able to
    decidualize and less able to form and hold a hemostatic plug. This node is where the
    reproductive and the bleeding phenotypes share a mechanism.

    Scope note: the purely physical contributions to the bleeding - increased endometrial
    surface area, disordered myometrial contractility, and venous ectasia from
    compression - are real and are cited below, but they are properties of the tumor mass
    and belong to the Tumor Growth and Bulk Compression nodes. This node is the
    endometrial signalling arm, which is why it is tagged TISSUE.
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: endometrial stromal cell
    term:
      id: CL:0002255
      label: stromal cell of endometrium
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: endometrial hemostatic plug formation
    term:
      id: GO:0007596
      label: blood coagulation
    modifier: DECREASED
  - preferred_term: endometrial decidualization
    term:
      id: GO:0046697
      label: decidualization
    modifier: DECREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "TGF-β3 secreted by fibroids has been shown to induce BMP-2 resistance in endometrium by down-regulation of BMPR-2, likely causing defective endometrial decidualization"
    explanation: >-
      Supports the decidualization arm: fibroid-derived TGF-beta-3 downregulates BMPR-2
      in the endometrium and impairs decidualization.
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "TGF-β3 also reduces expression of plasminogen activator inhibitor-1 (PAI-1), Antithrombin III (ATIII), and thrombomodulin in endometrium, likely contributing to bleeding"
    explanation: >-
      Supports the hemostatic arm: the same fibroid-derived TGF-beta-3 lowers three
      endometrial coagulation regulators, which the source links to the bleeding.
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Fibroids may cause HMB merely physically, i.e., through an increase in endometrial surface due to underlying fibroid growth; through an influence on normal myometrial contractility patterns; through an ulcerated or degenerating fibroid, or through uterine venous ectasia due to compression from the fibroids"
    explanation: >-
      Enumerates the mechanical contributions this node records alongside the
      TGF-beta-3-mediated ones, and is the reason the node is not described as purely
      biochemical.
  downstream:
  - target: Heavy Menstrual Bleeding
    description: >-
      The hemostatic and vascular failure described by this node is what makes the
      menstrual loss heavy.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:36303616
      reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "the heavy bleeding resulted from a failure of the fibrin/platelet plugs formed in the coagulation cascade in closing these blood vessels of increased diameter successfully"
      explanation: >-
        States the hemostatic failure to bleeding step directly.
  - target: Infertility and Reproductive Morbidity
    description: >-
      Defective decidualization is the shared step by which the same TGF-beta-3 signal
      that impairs hemostasis also impairs implantation.
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    intermediate_mechanisms:
    - BMPR-2 downregulation produces endometrial BMP-2 resistance.
    - BMP-2-resistant endometrium decidualizes defectively, impairing implantation.

- name: Tumor Growth and Clinical Manifestations
  description: >
    Progressive growth of the leiomyoma produces a benign smooth muscle tumor of the
    uterus. Submucosal and intramural tumors distort the endometrial cavity and impair
    hemostasis, causing heavy menstrual bleeding and consequent iron deficiency anemia.
    Bulk from larger tumors compresses adjacent pelvic organs (bladder, rectum), causing
    pressure symptoms, while distortion of the cavity and tubal ostia contributes to
    reproductive morbidity. Most affected women are asymptomatic: fibroids cause
    clinically significant disease in roughly a quarter of reproductive-age women
    overall, and affect quality of life in approximately 30% of cases.
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: leiomyoma smooth muscle cell
    term:
      id: CL:0000192
      label: smooth muscle cell
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40214304
    reference_title: UTERINE FIBROIDS.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Uterine fibroids (leiomyomas), the most common tumors in women and those \nassigned female at birth, originate from myometrial smooth muscle cells and \ncause heavy menstrual bleeding, anemia, pelvic discomfort, pregnancy loss, and \nobstruction of labor in approximately a quarter of reproductive-age women."
    explanation: >-
      Connects the smooth muscle tumor to its core clinical manifestations: heavy
      menstrual bleeding, anemia, pelvic discomfort, pregnancy loss, and obstruction
      of labor.
  - reference: PMID:38723935
    reference_title: The modern management of uterine fibroids-related abnormal uterine bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "in \napproximately 30% of cases, UFs affect the quality of life and women's health, \nwith abnormal uterine bleeding and heavy menstrual bleeding being the most \ncommon complaints, along with iron deficiency (ID) and ID anemia."
    explanation: >-
      Quantifies the symptomatic fraction and identifies AUB/HMB and iron deficiency
      anemia as the dominant complaints.
  downstream:
  - target: Bulk Compression of Adjacent Pelvic Organs
    description: >-
      Once the tumor is large enough, its mass alone displaces the neighbouring bladder
      and rectum; this arm of the mechanism is purely mechanical and is independent of
      the bleeding arm.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
  - target: Pelvic Mass
    description: >-
      A tumor large enough to enlarge the uterus becomes palpable.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:38929485
      reference_title: Currently Available Treatment Modalities for Uterine Fibroids.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "the most common benign \ngynecological condition in patients presenting with abnormal uterine bleeding, \npelvic masses causing pressure or pain"
      explanation: >-
        Names the pelvic mass as a presentation of the fibroid itself, which is the step
        this edge draws.
  - target: Pelvic Pain
    description: >-
      Bulk and pressure from the growing tumor produce pelvic pain and discomfort.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:40214304
      reference_title: UTERINE FIBROIDS.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "Uterine fibroids (leiomyomas), the most common tumors in women and those \nassigned female at birth, originate from myometrial smooth muscle cells and \ncause heavy menstrual bleeding, anemia, pelvic discomfort, pregnancy loss, and \nobstruction of labor in approximately a quarter of reproductive-age women."
      explanation: >-
        Names this manifestation among the consequences the review attributes directly to
        the fibroid, which is the step this edge draws.
  - target: Dysmenorrhea
    description: >-
      Disordered myometrial contractility around the tumor makes menstruation painful.
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    intermediate_mechanisms:
    - The tumor disturbs normal myometrial contractility patterns.

      reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
      supports: SUPPORT

      snippet: "through an influence on normal myometrial contractility patterns"
      explanation: >-
        Names the contractility disturbance that is the intermediate on this edge.
  - target: Dyspareunia
    description: >-
      The enlarged, deformed uterus makes intercourse painful, position-dependently.

      explanation: >-
        Supports the bulk-to-pain step in general terms without naming dyspareunia, which
        is why this is graded INDIRECT.
  - target: Endometrial Hemostatic Failure and Impaired Vessel Closure
    description: >-
      A cavity-distorting tumor is the precondition for the endometrial signalling and
      hemostatic changes that produce the bleeding.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT

- name: Bulk Compression of Adjacent Pelvic Organs
  description: >
    A fibroid large enough to enlarge and deform the uterus displaces the structures
    around it. Anterior and fundal tumors press on the bladder, producing urinary
    frequency and, when the ureters are involved, hydronephrosis; posterior tumors press
    on the rectum, producing constipation; a large fibroid can go further and cause
    urinary obstruction or, rarely, hydronephrosis. This is the one arm of the mechanism
    that is purely a consequence of volume and position: severity correlates with fibroid
    size, number and anatomical placement rather than with driver genotype, and the
    treatments that shrink or remove the tumor relieve it while the treatments that only
    control bleeding do not.
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The bulk growth of this extremely dense tissue leads an enlarged and deformed uterus and to some of the key symptoms associated with uterine fibroids in addition to HMB such as pressure symptoms, abdominal pain, and infertility"
    explanation: >-
      Attributes the pressure symptoms specifically to the bulk of the dense tumor
      enlarging and deforming the uterus, which is the mechanical claim of this node.
  - reference: PMID:40970558
    reference_title: Diagnosis and classification of uterine fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Fibroids positioned anteriorly or posteriorly can compress the bladder or rectum, resulting in urinary frequency, difficulty emptying the bladder, or constipation."
    explanation: >-
      States the compression mechanism and both of its outputs directly, which is the
      whole content of this node.
  - reference: PMID:40970558
    reference_title: Diagnosis and classification of uterine fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "In severe cases, particularly large fibroids may lead to urinary obstruction or rare hydronephrosis"
    explanation: >-
      Supplies the severe end of the compression spectrum named in this node's
      description, including the hydronephrosis that would otherwise be uncited.
  - reference: PMID:40970558
    reference_title: Diagnosis and classification of uterine fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The extent and severity of these symptoms correlate with fibroid size, number, and anatomical placement within the uterus."
    explanation: >-
      Names the three determinants of bulk-symptom severity recorded in this node's
      description, and is the reason it does not claim size alone.
  downstream:
  - target: Urinary Frequency
    description: >-
      Anterior and fundal tumors press on the bladder, reducing its capacity.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:40970558
      reference_title: Diagnosis and classification of uterine fibroids.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "Fibroids positioned anteriorly or posteriorly can compress the bladder or rectum, resulting in urinary frequency, difficulty emptying the bladder, or constipation."
      explanation: >-
        States bladder compression producing urinary frequency, which is exactly this edge.
  - target: Constipation from Bulk Compression
    description: >-
      Posterior tumors press on the rectum.
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:40970558
      reference_title: Diagnosis and classification of uterine fibroids.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "Fibroids positioned anteriorly or posteriorly can compress the bladder or rectum, resulting in urinary frequency, difficulty emptying the bladder, or constipation."
      explanation: >-
        Names rectal compression producing constipation, which is exactly this edge.

    directness: INDIRECT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Furthermore, when symptoms occur, they may not be related to the fibroids since abnormal uterine bleeding (AUB), infertility, recurrent pregnancy loss, pressure, pain, urinary frequency, or gastrointestinal dysfunction may be present with a variety of otherwise unrelated conditions."
    explanation: >-
      Kept deliberately as the source's own counter-caution: urinary frequency is not
      specific to fibroids and may belong to an unrelated condition in the same patient.
      Quoted in full including the "may not be related to the fibroids" clause so it
      cannot be read as attributing the symptom.

- category: Clinical
  name: Pelvic Mass
  description: >
    A palpable, often irregular pelvic or lower abdominal mass on examination when the
    fibroid uterus is sufficiently enlarged. Its diagnostic value is limited: fibroids
    are not reliably detected by palpation and their impact cannot be predicted from it,
    which is why imaging rather than examination anchors the diagnosis.
  phenotype_term:

- category: Clinical
  name: Recurrent Pregnancy Loss
  description: >
    Pregnancy loss in fibroid-complicated pregnancies. The cited meta-analysis reports
    miscarriage rather than recurrent miscarriage, and does not attribute it to cavity
    distortion, so neither qualifier is claimed here; the recurrent framing comes from
    the phenotype concept, not from this source. A meta-analysis of
    24 studies found fibroids raised miscarriage risk in unadjusted analysis; after
    adjustment for confounders the significant obstetric associations were preterm
    birth, cesarean delivery, placenta previa, placental abruption, postpartum
    hemorrhage, intrauterine fetal death, breech presentation and preeclampsia, and
    miscarriage was not among them. Both results are recorded here because the
    distinction matters for how strongly this phenotype should be claimed.

    The ontology binding is deliberately omitted. HP:0200067 Recurrent spontaneous
    abortion means exactly this, but it sits under HP:0032443 (past medical history)
    rather than under HP:0000118 (phenotypic abnormality), so it is not a member of the
    PhenotypeTerm dynamic enum; HP:0005268 Miscarriage and HP:0003826 Stillbirth are
    both outside the enum root too, under the clinical-modifier branch. Binding a term
    that means something else would be worse than leaving it unbound, so the meaning is
    carried by preferred_term. Asherman_Syndrome records the same gap for the same term
    (issue #7837, gap 3).
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Recurrent spontaneous abortion
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38710995
    reference_title: "The influence of uterine fibroids on adverse outcomes in pregnant women: a meta-analysis."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The pooled results showed that fibroids elevated the risk of adverse outcomes, including preterm birth, cesarean delivery, placenta previa, miscarriage, preterm premature rupture of membranes (PPROM), placental abruption, postpartum hemorrhage (PPH), fetal distress, malposition, intrauterine fetal death, low birth weight, breech presentation, and preeclampsia."
    explanation: >-
      The pooled unadjusted meta-analysis result includes miscarriage among the
      fibroid-associated adverse outcomes.
  - reference: PMID:38710995
    reference_title: "The influence of uterine fibroids on adverse outcomes in pregnant women: a meta-analysis."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "However, after adjusting for the potential factors, negative effects were only seen for preterm birth, cesarean delivery, placenta previa, placental abruption, PPH, intrauterine fetal death, breech presentation, and preeclampsia."
    explanation: >-
      The same meta-analysis's adjusted analysis does not retain miscarriage, so this
      sentence contradicts the unadjusted association above. Recorded as a separate
      REFUTE item rather than folded into the first, because one evidence item cannot
      carry two opposite directions.

    Delivery before 37 weeks in pregnancies complicated by fibroids. Unlike miscarriage,

  phenotype_term:

    explanation: >-
      Preterm birth is first in the list of associations that remain significant after
      adjustment, which is the stronger claim this phenotype rests on.

genetic:
- name: MED12 somatic exon-2 mutation
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: MED12
    term:
      id: hgnc:11957
      label: MED12
  variant_origin: SOMATIC
  features: >
    Recurrent somatic exon-2 (codon 44 region) point mutations in MED12 are the most
    common driver, present in ~50-80% of uterine fibroids (~77% in a large review).
    These gain-of-function mutations disrupt the CDK8/CDK19 Mediator kinase module,
    are more frequent in Black women, are commonly found even in small tumors, and
    associate with multiple rather than solitary fibroids. Note that the mutation is not
    simply growth-promoting: MED12 wild-type leiomyomas have a higher growth potential
    than mutant ones and show higher MED12-associated CDK8 kinase activity, which the
    entry records as an open question rather than smoothing over.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36835153
    reference_title: Differential Expression of MED12-Associated Coding RNA Transcripts in Uterine Leiomyomas.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Recent studies have demonstrated that somatic MED12 mutations in exon 2 occur at \na frequency of up to 80% and have a functional role in leiomyoma pathogenesis."
    explanation: Documents somatic MED12 exon-2 mutations at up to 80% frequency.
  - reference: PMID:37668348
    reference_title: "RISING STARS: Role of MED12 mutation in the pathogenesis of uterine fibroids."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "They are gain-of-function \nmutations and are more frequently observed in Black women and commonly observed \neven in small UFs."
    explanation: Characterizes MED12 mutations as gain-of-function and more frequent in Black women.
  - reference: PMID:41493967
    reference_title: Impact of MED12 mutation and CDK8 activity on uterine leiomyoma growth and response to gonadotropin-releasing hormone agonist treatment.
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "MED12 wild-type leiomyomas have a higher growth potential than mutant leiomyomas, suggesting that the mutation limits leiomyoma growth."
    explanation: >-
      Contradicts the reading of MED12 mutation as straightforwardly growth-promoting
      that a gain-of-function label invites: wild-type tumors grow faster. Recorded as
      REFUTE against that stronger claim rather than omitted, and followed up in the
      discussions section.

- name: HMGA2 overexpression / rearrangement

  variant_origin: SOMATIC
  features: >

    leiomyoma variants).

    explanation: >-
      Documents HMGA2 overexpression and rearrangement frequencies in cellular
      leiomyoma.

- name: FH (fumarate hydratase) deficiency
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: FH
    term:
      id: hgnc:3700
      label: FH
  variant_origin: GERMLINE_AND_SOMATIC
  features: >
    Biallelic FH loss defines the fumarate-hydratase-deficient leiomyoma subtype. In an
    80-case series the first FH hit was germline in 31 tumors, somatic in 29, and
    undetected by exon sequencing in 20, so the subtype is not synonymous with the
    hereditary syndrome. Germline FH variants cause hereditary leiomyomatosis and renal
    cell cancer (HLRCC), an autosomal dominant syndrome of cutaneous and uterine
    leiomyomas plus renal cell carcinoma, which dismech curates separately as
    Hereditary_Leiomyomatosis_and_Renal_Cell_Cancer; 69% of the germline-variant
    patients in that series had a clear family history. Because the germline case is
    heritable and carries a renal cancer risk, recognizing FH-deficient histology on a
    resected fibroid is the clinically consequential part of this subtype.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40862769
    reference_title: The Roles of Non-Coding RNAs in the Pathogenesis of Uterine Fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Driver mutations in genes such as \nMED12, HMGA2, and FH also play roles in the development and growth of fibroids."
    explanation: Lists FH among recurrent fibroid driver genes.
  - reference: PMID:36981015
    reference_title: "A Missense Mutation c.1132G > A in Fumarate Hydratase (FH) Leads to Hereditary Leiomyomatosis and Renal Cell Cancer (HLRCC) Syndrome and Insights into Clinical Management in Uterine Leiomyomata."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "HLRCC syndrome is a hereditary cancer predisposition syndrome caused by heterozygous germline pathogenic variant of the fumarate hydratase (FH) gene and characterized by cutaneous leiomyomas (CL), uterine leiomyomas (UL), and renal cell carcinoma (RCC)."
    explanation: >-
      Supplies the citation the previous version of this record said was missing: a
      heterozygous germline FH variant causes HLRCC, whose features include uterine
      leiomyoma alongside cutaneous leiomyoma and renal cell carcinoma.
  - reference: PMID:37263921
    reference_title: "[Fumarate hydratase deficient uterine leiomyoma: a clinicopathological and molecular analysis of 80 cases]."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Sixty-nine percent (20/29) of the patients with FH germline variation had clear family history."
    explanation: >-
      Quantifies the family-history rate among germline-FH patients, supporting the
      claim that identifying this subtype has consequences beyond the index tumor.
  - reference: PMID:37263921
    reference_title: "[Fumarate hydratase deficient uterine leiomyoma: a clinicopathological and molecular analysis of 80 cases]."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Patients' age ranged from 18 to 54 (36.0±7.5) years, with more than 60% exhibiting clinical symptoms of multiple and large leiomyomas (the median diameter was 70 mm)."
    explanation: >-
      Characterizes the clinical presentation of the FH-deficient subtype as multiple,
      large (median 70 mm) and symptomatic in the majority.

histopathology:
- name: Whorled fascicles of spindle smooth muscle cells in collagenous stroma
  description: >
    The conventional leiomyoma is a well-circumscribed nodule of bland spindle smooth
    muscle cells arranged in interlacing whorled fascicles, separated from the
    surrounding myometrium by a cleft, and set in an abundant collagen-rich stroma that
    accounts for much of the tumor's volume. Histology is confirmatory rather than
    diagnostic in routine practice, because fibroids are diagnosed on imaging and
    tissue is normally examined only after resection.
  finding_term:
    preferred_term: uterine corpus leiomyoma
    term:
      id: NCIT:C3434
      label: Uterine Corpus Leiomyoma
  diagnostic: true
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Despite the name, fibroids largely comprise of myocytes rather than fibroblasts and are characterized by the excessive deposition of extracellular matrix substances, mainly collagen, within the tumor"
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the two components of this finding: a tumor made of smooth muscle
      cells rather than fibroblasts, in an excess of mainly collagenous matrix.
  - reference: PMID:36303616
    reference_title: Uterine Fibroids (Leiomyomata) and Heavy Menstrual Bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "it is separated from the myometrium by a clear cleft, as observed in histological images"
    explanation: >-
      Supports the circumscription and the cleft that separate the nodule from the
      adjacent myometrium on histology.

- name: FH-deficient histologic signature
  subtype: FH-deficient
  description: >
    A recognizable constellation rather than a single feature: staghorn vasculature,
    alveolar-pattern oedema, bizarre nuclei, oval nuclei arranged in chains, prominent
    eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes, and eosinophilic intracytoplasmic
    globules. More than four of these were present in 98.5% of an 80-case series.
    Confirmation is immunohistochemical: loss of FH staining (97.3% sensitive) or gain of
    S-(2-succino)-cysteine staining (100% in the five cases tested). Finding this pattern
    is what prompts germline FH testing, so it is the histology with the largest
    downstream consequence in this disease.
  finding_term:
    preferred_term: FH-deficient uterine leiomyoma with bizarre nuclei
    term:
      id: NCIT:C4257
      label: Bizarre Leiomyoma
  frequency: VERY_FREQUENT
  diagnostic: true
  notes: >-
    The bound term names only the bizarre-nuclei component. NCIT has no term for the
    FH-deficient composite signature, and the atypical/bizarre leiomyoma concept is not
    coextensive with it, so preferred_term carries the specific meaning per the ontology
    term contract. The frequency band is within the FH-deficient subtype (98.5% of
    cases), not within uterine leiomyoma as a whole, where the subtype is uncommon.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:37263921
    reference_title: "[Fumarate hydratase deficient uterine leiomyoma: a clinicopathological and molecular analysis of 80 cases]."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "More than four histologic features, including staghorn vasculature, alveolar-pattern edema, bizarre nuclei, oval nuclei arranged in chains, prominent eosinophilic nucleoli with perinucleolar haloes and eosinophilic intracytoplasmic globules were observed in 98.5% (67/68) patients."
    explanation: >-
      Lists the exact constellation and its frequency within the FH-deficient series.
  - reference: PMID:37263921
    reference_title: "[Fumarate hydratase deficient uterine leiomyoma: a clinicopathological and molecular analysis of 80 cases]."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "FH immunohistochemistry were performed in 74 cases; S-(2-succino)-cysteine (2SC) were also detected by immunohistochemistry in five cases."
    explanation: >-
      Documents the immunohistochemical confirmation step, and the small number of 2SC
      cases behind its reported 100% sensitivity.

- name: Cellular leiomyoma variant
  subtype: HMGA2-rearranged
  description: >
    A histologic variant with markedly increased cellularity and correspondingly less
    stroma, which raises the differential with endometrial stromal tumors and
    leiomyosarcoma. Molecularly it is enriched for HMGA2 aberration, then chromosome 1p
    deletion, then MED12 mutation, so the variant is not just a morphologic curiosity
    but tracks a different driver distribution from conventional leiomyoma.
  finding_term:
    preferred_term: uterine corpus cellular leiomyoma
    term:
      id: NCIT:C40163
      label: Uterine Corpus Cellular Leiomyoma
  evidence:

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: "36.5% showed HMGA2 overexpression at the IHC \nlevel, associated with increased mRNA expression in 14/14 cases."
    explanation: >-
      Supports the enrichment of HMGA2 aberration in this histologic variant.

environmental:
- name: Chronic low vitamin D status
  description: >
    Vitamin D deficiency is repeatedly associated with fibroid presence and size, and
    unlike most epidemiological risk factors it has a specific proposed mechanism at the
    initiating node rather than at the growth node: calcitriol acting through VDR
    supports the MRN-complex/ATM DNA-repair axis in myometrial stem cells, so deficiency
    plausibly permits the driver lesion to be acquired and retained. In vitro work shows
    vitamin D inhibiting fibroid growth through proliferation, ECM remodelling, DNA
    repair and apoptosis pathways; clinical studies support this only in part.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: vitamin D deficiency
  effect: Risk factor for fibroid development and growth; adequate status is plausibly protective.
  review_notes: >-
    exposure_term is left ontology-unbound deliberately. ECTO was searched
    (sqlite:obo:ecto) for "vitamin D deficiency" and for deficiency-shaped exposure
    terms and returned nothing; the nearest terms, ECTO:9000133 "exposure to vitamin D"
    and XCO:0000543 "vitamin D", both name the presence of the exposure, which is the
    opposite of the risk factor recorded here. No term beats a term that means the
    inverse, so preferred_term carries the meaning.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:33670322
    reference_title: "Vitamin D: Mechanism of Action and Biological Effects in Uterine Fibroids."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "In recent years, epidemiological studies reported a vitamin D deficiency in patients with UFs raised interest in the potential biological effects of vitamin D supplementation."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the epidemiological association between vitamin D deficiency and
      uterine fibroids that this exposure record is about.
  - reference: PMID:33670322
    reference_title: "Vitamin D: Mechanism of Action and Biological Effects in Uterine Fibroids."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "In vitro studies proved vitamin D efficacy in inhibiting UFs growth by targeting pathways involved in the regulation of various biological processes, including proliferation, extracellular matrix (ECM) remodeling, DNA repair, signaling and apoptosis."
    explanation: >-
      Supports the mechanistic half of the claim, naming the DNA-repair and ECM pathways
      this record links to the pathograph.
  - reference: PMID:33670322
    reference_title: "Vitamin D: Mechanism of Action and Biological Effects in Uterine Fibroids."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "However, clinical studies supported only in part the beneficial effects of vitamin D supplementation in reducing UFs growth and tumor volume."
    explanation: >-
      Contradicts the stronger reading that supplementation is an established
      fibroid-directed treatment. Kept as a separate REFUTE item because the same source
      supports the association and undercuts the therapeutic claim.
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation
    environmental_effect: PREDISPOSES
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Low vitamin D status is linked to the initiating lesion rather than to hormonal
      growth: VDR signalling supports the MRN-complex/ATM DNA-damage response in
      myometrial stem cells, and its loss leaves DNA damage unrepaired in exactly the
      cell population from which fibroids arise.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:32545544
      reference_title: Vitamin D3 Ameliorates DNA Damage Caused by Developmental Exposure to Endocrine Disruptors in the Uterine Myometrial Stem Cells of Eker Rats.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: "VitD3 acts on DNA repair via the MRN complex/ATM axis, restores the DNA repair signaling network, and enhances DDR."
      explanation: >-
        Names the repair axis vitamin D acts through in myometrial stem cells, which is
        the intermediate this edge asserts between vitamin D status and the driver lesion.

- name: Developmental exposure to endocrine-disrupting chemicals
  description: >
    Early-life exposure of the myometrium to endocrine-disrupting chemicals raises adult
    fibroid risk. The strongest mechanistic evidence is from Eker rats exposed neonatally
    to diethylstilbestrol, whose myometrial stem cells show reduced RAD50 and MRE11 and a
    defective DNA damage response at the pro-fibroid stage, months before tumors appear.
    In humans the corresponding exposures reported are xenoestrogens in early
    ontogenesis, diethylstilbestrol, and phthalates and bisphenol A.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: exposure to xenoestrogen
    term:
      id: ECTO:9002036
      label: exposure to xenoestrogen
  effect: Developmental risk factor, acting through DNA-damage-response impairment in myometrial stem cells.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36982852
    reference_title: Update on the Role and Regulatory Mechanism of Extracellular Matrix in the Pathogenesis of Uterine Fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "These factors include race, age, parity, family history, food additives, obesity, vitamin D deficiency, and endocrine-disrupting chemical exposure"
    explanation: >-
      Human review listing endocrine-disrupting chemical exposure among the established
      fibroid risk factors. Cited here rather than the opening sentence of the Eker-rat
      paper, which asserts the same association but is a rodent study and so cannot carry
      a HUMAN_CLINICAL grade.
  - reference: PMID:32545544
    reference_title: Vitamin D3 Ameliorates DNA Damage Caused by Developmental Exposure to Endocrine Disruptors in the Uterine Myometrial Stem Cells of Eker Rats.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "Results showed that there was a significantly reduced expression of RAD50 and MRE11, key DNA repair proteins in DES-exposed myometrial tissues, compared to vehicle (VEH)-exposed tissues (p < 0.01)."
    explanation: >-
      The rat experimental result behind the mechanism: developmental DES exposure
      depletes two MRN-complex repair proteins in myometrium.
  - reference: PMID:36982825
    reference_title: "A View on Uterine Leiomyoma Genesis through the Prism of Genetic, Epigenetic and Cellular Heterogeneity."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The risk factors include advanced reproductive age, African and Latin American ethnicity, UL cases in family medical history, obesity, early menarche, arterial hypertension, chronic inflammation, exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, sexually transmitted infections and alcohol"
    explanation: >-
      Independent review listing exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis among
      the established risk factors, which is the exposure term bound here.
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Driver Mutation-Initiated Clonal Transformation
    environmental_effect: PREDISPOSES
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Developmental xenoestrogen exposure leaves the myometrial stem cell pool
      repair-deficient, which is how an exposure decades earlier raises the probability
      of the somatic driver lesion that starts a fibroid.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:32545544
      reference_title: Vitamin D3 Ameliorates DNA Damage Caused by Developmental Exposure to Endocrine Disruptors in the Uterine Myometrial Stem Cells of Eker Rats.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: "Results showed that there was a significantly reduced expression of RAD50 and MRE11, key DNA repair proteins in DES-exposed myometrial tissues, compared to vehicle (VEH)-exposed tissues (p < 0.01)."
      explanation: >-
        Supplies the intermediate step - repair-protein loss in the exposed myometrium -
        that links the developmental exposure to the initiating lesion.

- name: Obesity and excess adiposity
  description: >
    Obesity is one of the most consistently reported fibroid risk factors, and the
    proposed mechanism is specific: adipose tissue performs the same aromatase and
    17-beta-hydroxysteroid-dehydrogenase conversion of androgens to oestrogens that
    fibroid cells themselves use, so excess adiposity raises the oestrogen supply to a
    tumor that depends on it. This is why the edge below attaches to the hormone-driven
    proliferation node rather than to the initiating lesion.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: obesity and high body mass index
  effect: Risk factor for fibroid development, acting through increased peripheral oestrogen synthesis.
  review_notes: >-
    exposure_term is left ontology-unbound deliberately. ECTO and XCO were both searched
    (sqlite:obo:ecto, sqlite:obo:xco) for "obesity", "body mass" and "high fat diet" and
    returned no term. Obesity is a host state rather than an exposure, so the absence is
    expected rather than a gap in those ontologies, and preferred_term carries it.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36982825
    reference_title: "A View on Uterine Leiomyoma Genesis through the Prism of Genetic, Epigenetic and Cellular Heterogeneity."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The risk factors include advanced reproductive age, African and Latin American ethnicity, UL cases in family medical history, obesity, early menarche, arterial hypertension, chronic inflammation, exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, sexually transmitted infections and alcohol"
    explanation: >-
      Lists obesity among the established risk factors for uterine leiomyoma.
  - reference: PMID:36982825
    reference_title: "A View on Uterine Leiomyoma Genesis through the Prism of Genetic, Epigenetic and Cellular Heterogeneity."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The enzymatic conversion of androgens to oestrogens is also typical of fat tissue"
    explanation: >-
      Gives the mechanism this record attributes the obesity association to, and is the
      reason the pathograph edge targets the hormone node.
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation
    environmental_effect: PREDISPOSES
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Adipose aromatase activity adds to the circulating oestrogen pool that the
      established fibroid clone depends on for growth.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:36982825
      reference_title: "A View on Uterine Leiomyoma Genesis through the Prism of Genetic, Epigenetic and Cellular Heterogeneity."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "The enzymatic conversion of androgens to oestrogens is also typical of fat tissue"
      explanation: >-
        Names the aromatization step by which adiposity feeds the oestrogen-dependent
        growth node.

- name: Alcohol consumption
  description: >
    Alcohol appears in the risk-factor lists of contemporary reviews of uterine
    leiomyoma genesis. No mechanism specific to fibroids is established for it, so it is
    recorded as an association and its pathograph edge is left off rather than attached
    to a node on speculation.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: exposure to alcohol consumption
    term:
      id: ECTO:0001082
      label: exposure to alcohol consumption
  effect: Reported risk factor for uterine leiomyoma development.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36982825
    reference_title: "A View on Uterine Leiomyoma Genesis through the Prism of Genetic, Epigenetic and Cellular Heterogeneity."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The risk factors include advanced reproductive age, African and Latin American ethnicity, UL cases in family medical history, obesity, early menarche, arterial hypertension, chronic inflammation, exposure to xenoestrogens in early ontogenesis, sexually transmitted infections and alcohol"
    explanation: >-
      Lists alcohol last among the risk factors for uterine leiomyoma in this review.

- name: Parity
  description: >
    Having borne children is consistently reported as protective, in contrast to the
    nulliparity and early-menarche exposures that raise risk. Parity, oral contraceptive
    use and smoking are the three protective factors this review names; smoking is
    recorded there too but is not curated here as a protective exposure, because a
    protective association that nobody would act on is a poor fit for a slot that reads
    as an intervention.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: parity
  effect: Protective factor against uterine leiomyoma development.
  review_notes: >-
    exposure_term is left ontology-unbound deliberately. Parity is a reproductive history
    attribute, not an environmental exposure, and ECTO has no term for it; it is curated
    here because the Environmental class is where dismech records risk and protective
    factors, and dropping it would lose a consistently reported protective association.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36982825
    reference_title: "A View on Uterine Leiomyoma Genesis through the Prism of Genetic, Epigenetic and Cellular Heterogeneity."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Parity, oral contraceptive intake and smoking are among possible protective factors for UL development"
    explanation: >-
      Names parity first among the possible protective factors for uterine leiomyoma.
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation
    environmental_effect: PROTECTS_AGAINST
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Parity interrupts the sequence of ovulatory cycles that supplies the oestrogen and
      progesterone this node depends on. The intermediates are not established, which is
      why the link type says so rather than naming a pathway.

treatments:
- name: GnRH Agonist Therapy
  therapeutic_modality: PEPTIDE
  description: >
    GnRH agonists (e.g., leuprolide) cause pituitary downregulation and hypoestrogenism,
    producing temporary fibroid shrinkage and bleeding control. Used as a preoperative
    bridge; limited by menopausal symptoms, bone loss, and rebound growth after stopping.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: leuprolide
      term:
        id: CHEBI:6427
        label: leuprolide
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38929485
    reference_title: Currently Available Treatment Modalities for Uterine Fibroids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "available medications (progestogens, combined \noral contraceptives andgonadotropin-releasing hormone agonists and antagonists) \nare generally used for short-term treatment of fibroid-induced bleeding."
    explanation: Documents GnRH agonists/antagonists for short-term fibroid bleeding control.

- name: Oral GnRH Antagonist Therapy
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  description: >
    Oral GnRH antagonists (elagolix, relugolix, linzagolix) directly block pituitary GnRH
    receptors to suppress ovarian steroid production, reducing heavy menstrual bleeding
    in a uterus-sparing, reversible regimen, usually with steroid add-back to avoid the
    hypoestrogenic effects that limit GnRH agonists. Linzagolix is the one agent with a
    lower-dose arm usable without mandatory add-back. The effect is suppressive, not
    curative: symptoms recur relatively quickly after the drug is stopped, which is the
    argument the PRIMROSE withdrawal study makes for long-term continuation.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: elagolix
      term:
        id: NCIT:C153373
        label: Elagolix
    - preferred_term: relugolix
      term:
        id: NCIT:C114498
        label: Relugolix
    - preferred_term: linzagolix
      term:
        id: NCIT:C148479
        label: Linzagolix
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38723935
    reference_title: The modern management of uterine fibroids-related abnormal uterine bleeding.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "hormonal therapies, including combined oral contraceptives, \ngonadotropin-releasing hormone agonists or antagonists"
    explanation: Documents GnRH antagonists among hormonal therapies for fibroid-related AUB.
  - reference: PMID:33596357
    reference_title: Treatment of Uterine Fibroid Symptoms with Relugolix Combination Therapy.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A total of 73% of the participants in the relugolix combination therapy group in trial L1 and 71% of those in trial L2 had a response (primary end point), as compared with 19% and 15%, respectively, of those in the placebo groups (P<0.001 for both comparisons)."
    explanation: >-
      The LIBERTY 1 and 2 phase-3 result quantifying the bleeding response to relugolix
      plus oestradiol and norethindrone against placebo, which is the trial evidence
      behind this treatment class.
  - reference: PMID:33596357
    reference_title: Treatment of Uterine Fibroid Symptoms with Relugolix Combination Therapy.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Treatment with the combination of relugolix (an oral gonadotropin-releasing hormone-receptor antagonist), estradiol, and norethindrone acetate, administered once daily, may have efficacy in women with uterine fibroids and heavy bleeding while avoiding hypoestrogenic effects."
    explanation: >-
      States the rationale for the add-back component this treatment record describes:
      the steroid add-back is what makes long-term GnRH-receptor blockade tolerable.
  - reference: PMID:40543832
    reference_title: "Linzagolix with and without hormonal add-back therapy for symptomatic uterine fibroids: PRIMROSE 1 & 2 long-term extension and withdrawal study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The relatively fast recurrence of uterine fibroid-associated symptoms after cessation of therapy is an argument in favor of long-term continuation of treatment."
    explanation: >-
      The PRIMROSE withdrawal result, and the source of this record's statement that the
      effect is suppressive rather than curative: symptoms return quickly once the drug
      stops, which is why the class is framed as long-term therapy.

- name: Selective Progesterone Receptor Modulator Therapy
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  description: >
    Selective progesterone receptor modulators (SPRMs, e.g., ulipristal acetate)
    modulate PGR signaling to reduce bleeding and fibroid size; intermittent long-term
    use shows good results but is limited by hepatotoxicity concerns. Response may vary
    with MED12 mutation status.
  treatment_term:

    explanation: Documents intermittent SPRM therapy reducing bleeding and fibroid size.

- name: Mifepristone
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  description: >
    Antiprogestin studied for symptomatic fibroids, reducing abnormal uterine bleeding
    and fibroid-related dysmenorrhea. It acts on the same progesterone-receptor axis as
    the selective progesterone receptor modulators curated above, and is grouped with
    them mechanistically rather than with the symptomatic agents; its safety profile is
    described as more favourable than ulipristal acetate's but less well established.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: mifepristone
      term:
        id: CHEBI:50692
        label: mifepristone
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Hormone-Driven Myometrial Smooth Muscle Proliferation
    description: >-
      Blocking the progesterone receptor removes the paracrine WNT and growth-factor
      signal that this node depends on, which is the mechanism the bleeding and
      dysmenorrhea response is attributed to.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39335725
    reference_title: "Tailoring the Diagnostic Pathway for Medical and Surgical Treatment of Uterine Fibroids: A Narrative Review."
    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-
      Documents mifepristone's efficacy for both of the symptoms this treatment record
      claims, in a review of the fibroid treatment pathway.

- name: Iron Replacement Therapy
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  description: >
    Repletes iron stores in fibroid-related iron deficiency and anemia from chronic
    bleeding; an important adjunct before and after surgery and during medical therapy.

    therapeutic_agent is deliberately absent. The treatment is generic iron replacement,
    oral or intravenous, and the preparation is not specified by the cited source.
    NCIT:C210688 "Iron Supplement" is the concept but is not a member of the
    ChemicalEntityTerm dynamic enum, and every CHEBI term that is a member names one
    specific salt or hydrate (for example CHEBI:75832, iron(2+) sulfate anhydrous), which
    would assert a preparation this entry does not claim.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: supportive care

      id: NCIT:C15747
      label: Supportive Care
